401. **(2026-11-08) 399 corrected: Utsuho's cone of light follows the player until the first bullet comes out.** The user: the cone stands still until the hole shoots. 399 chose the jets' way as the hole began to pull, but what it takes in comes out bit by bit through its whole time, the first a quarter of the way through: the cone stood still that long. And the way every bullet it took in would go was worked out as it was taken. Now what it takes in waits for its moment (`HoleAct.waiting`) and is shot out then, the way the jets go at that moment; the cone follows the player until the first one comes out, the way is chosen right then (`chosenAt`), and they turn away from there.
402. **(2026-11-08) 399 and 401 corrected: for Marisa and Utsuho, "direction" is the way round they turn, chosen once shooting begins.** The user: the cone of light should stand still before the hole shoots; Marisa shoots her beam in one direction and chooses its spin direction right when it is fully grown. Utsuho: the cone (and the jets) is aimed at where the player is when the warning appears and stands still there (it followed the player until the first bullet came out); when the first bullet comes out the way round the jets turn is chosen, towards the player from the nearer jet (new `HoleAct.turnWay`; it was always the spinner's way, `Period.AngularSpeed`), and they turn that way at an eighth of a spinner beam's speed, 90 degrees at most, as before. Marisa: her Master Spark is aimed at the player as it starts and keeps that way while it grows (it followed the player while growing: `LaserEmitter.AimsUntilGrown` is gone); once grown it chooses which way round to turn (the way the player is nearer right then, `TurnWay`, as already) and turns towards the player 5 degrees a second, always that way round.
403. **(2026-11-08) Murasa: her own anchors half as often.** The anchor thrown at the player (her own bullets, in a spinner too) comes every eight beats (`MURASA_AIMED_EVERY_BEATS` 4 -> 8). The eight round her every sixteen beats are her mechanic and stay. Read "her own anchor attack" as the aimed ones (the other reading: the sequences round her half as often too). Stars stay 4.
404. **(2026-11-08) Cut pieces no longer vanish and come back.** Every cut (Kagerou's and Elly's claws, Youmu's slashes, Ran's spirals: `CuttingAct.DrawPieces`) drew only the 400 newest pieces that were still alive (`CLAW_PIECES_DRAWN`), and pieces that had already flown off the screen counted among them: when many were cut an older piece still on the screen vanished, and came back out of nowhere when newer, faster pieces (shorter lives) ran out. Now every piece on the screen is drawn and what has left it is skipped (`CLAW_PIECES_DRAWN` gone). A piece is also drawn on its own clock, as the hidden bullet that hits is moved (`stageClock`): in another boss's stop or slowdown it was drawn flying on while what hurt stood still. Chirality [Asymmetry] 3:00 + 15 s: Kagerou 1.81 ms a frame, Elly 4.94, Youmu 4.85, Ran 2.05.
405. **(2026-11-08) Raiko: what her ripples swell hurts like a slider body.** A ring's bullet that a ripple has swollen at least half way (`RAIKO_SLIDER_FROM` 0.5 of `RAIKO_RIPPLE_GROW`) hurts like a slider body while it is so (not used up, the slider damage every interval, as a bullet in Sannyo's smoke: `collideRing`); what keeps its size (Utsuho's balls, trains) is not swollen. Stage bullets were never swollen by the ripples and are not touched. Difficulty 2 -> 3 stars (a slider tick is an eighth of the bar against a twelfth, and the bullet stays).
406. **(2026-11-08) Koishi and Satori: twice as many hearts.** Every ring of their hearts has twice the bullets (new `KOISHI_HEART_COUNT_FACTOR` 2): in their attacks the boss's count factor (Koishi's waves and Satori's flowers 14 -> 28 a ring), in a spinner every heart ring twice the spinner's stars (at most 64). Read "Komeiji" as both sisters, since Satori's own bullets are "hearts, as Koishi's" and share the code (the other reading: only one of them). Stars stay 4.
407. **(2026-11-08) Miko: one and a half times the cones, fainter at first.** `MIKO_CONES` 20 -> 30. A cone comes up half as visible (new `MIKO_CONE_START_ALPHA` 0.0875, half of `MIKO_CONE_ALPHA` 0.175: "starting transparency doubled" read as fainter, as before) and grows back to 0.175 evenly over its first six beats, until it starts to brighten towards the beams as before.
408. **(2026-11-08) Futo: the user's ship, twice as long, and nothing stays in it.** The boat is the user's picture of a wooden ship seen from above (360 x 126, a grey and white checkerboard drawn into it): new Bake mode `boat` takes the checkerboard away from the border inwards (so the light things on the deck stay), unblends the edge pixels from it, turns it bow to the right, cuts it to what shows and makes it 4 times as big with Lanczos: `Resources/Textures/futo_boat.png`, 1352 x 404 (source kept as `..\Futo_boat.png`); the drawn hull (`makeBoat`) is gone. Twice as long: 20 circle radii (`FUTO_BOAT_CIRCLES` 5 -> 10), as wide as the picture is for that, about 6 circle radii (`FUTO_BOAT_WIDTH_CIRCLES` gone; it was 2.5 wide; the other reading, keeping the old 4 : 1 shape and stretching the picture, was not taken). Its shape to touch and push out of is the picture's outline (top and bottom edge at 49 places along it, `StageLayer.BoatOutline`), no longer a capsule: the capsule's round ends left the corners of the flat stern free, where bullets sat in the drawn boat. It hurts where the picture is, as Murasa's anchor. A bullet whose body is in the hull is no longer put on the edge at once: it slides out to the nearest edge a little every step, at twice the boat's and its own speed together (new `FUTO_PUSH_SPEED` 2), and flies on its way as fast. A boat is looked at until all of it has left the screen (it went by the life of a bullet). Still not pushed, as before: what cannot be turned at all (a ring another boss's ops steer, homing and falling ones) and the acts' own bullets. Chirality [Asymmetry] 3:00 + 15 s: 2.00 ms a frame (the boat's pushes 0.99), with Kagerou 2.68.
409. **(2026-11-08) No limit on the big pictures.** The round bullets drawn sharp had no limit already (240), but what goes with them did: each one's two glows went into the layer under the bullets (1024 sprites for everything there: Sunny's prism, Nue's cones, Raiko's drums, Futo's boat...), and what did not fit was left out. Every sprite layer of the stage now takes as many as it is given (`StageLayer.Grows`): the layer under the bullets, Mokou's balls (96), her circles (96), the bats (512), Yuyuko's petals (1024), the planets, UFOs and heads (160), the black hole (96) and the World's Ender's Earth and its pieces (220). With 404 the cut pieces have no limit either.
410. **(2026-11-08) Marisa stands half as far from the middle.** Where a boss stands (opposite the player, through the middle of the screen) but half as far from the middle (new `MARISA_PLACE_SCALE` 0.5, `marisaPlace`), before the bosses are spread round each other, so that two bosses only share a circle when they really stand on each other; with three, her half-way place is one of her own. She still stands still while a Master Spark fires (the other reading of "position is default", that she should follow the player then too, was not taken).
411. **(2026-11-08) Song select sorts, groups and filters Danmakusu by its own stars.** The user asked whether Harmony could keep the Danmakusu star rating and sort by it. osu! sorts by difficulty, groups by difficulty and filters by stars ("stars>5", the star range of the settings) with the number it keeps in its database, osu!standard's for an osu! map (mania's for a mania map); the panels already showed the Danmakusu stars, worked out live by osu!'s difficulty cache with our calculator. New `Stars/`: `DanmakusuStars` keeps the Danmakusu stars by the map's MD5 in `danmakusu_stars.csv` in osu!'s data folder (md5, stars, `StarRating.STORE_VERSION` 1: raise it when the stars come out differently, and every map is rated again; osu!'s database is never written). `StarFiller` (in the ruleset icon, as `DefaultBossMod`) rates every map of the library that has no rating of this version in the background, osu! maps first, then taiko, catch and mania, on a quarter of the cores at the lowest priority, waiting while anything is played, and looks for new maps every two minutes; a map that cannot be rated is marked so and keeps osu!'s number. Every rating osu! has the calculator make without a mod that changes it (only boss selection mods) is kept too (`DanmakusuDifficultyCalculator`). `SongSelectStars` (Harmony, the embedded copy of `OtherModesInSongSelect`, now shared through `EnsureHarmony`): while Danmakusu is the mode, the difficulty sort is osu!'s own done with the Danmakusu stars (a prefix on `BeatmapCarouselFilterSorting.Run`, not on its small comparers, which the JIT may inline past a patch), the difficulty groups likewise (`getGroups`), and the star filters are checked by them while everything else of the filter is osu!'s check with its star filters taken out (`CheckCriteriaMatch`); a map not rated yet keeps osu!'s number. While new ratings come in song select sorts again every two minutes, and when a round is over, if it shows Danmakusu and goes by stars (`Resort`, the carousel caught as it is made). New `sectioncheck sortstars` (six made-up maps whose Danmakusu stars run against osu!'s, one unrated): in Danmakusu sorted 4, 3, 2, 1, 5, 0 with groups, filters and range by them; in osu!standard 0..5 as before. Not yet seen in osu!.
412. **(2026-11-08) Song select: bullets, beams and most bosses instead of circles and sliders; sorts by them; a column of Danmakusu's mods.** The user: show bullet and laser beam counts instead of circle and slider counts, the most Touhou bosses too, new sorts in the dropdown, and a Danmakusu row in the mods list. (1) Counts: the converter makes a `Stars/DanmakusuBeatmap` (osu!'s `OsuBeatmap` otherwise), whose statistics are Bullets, Beams (what the map's own objects make, planned without bosses as the stars are), Spinners and Most bosses (`BulletPlanner.MostBosses`, as the boss menu's Max), with bars full at 120 bullets a second, 160 beams a minute, 30 bosses (`STAT_*_FULL`; the user's hard example maps: 46-118, 46-154, 11-26). They are kept with the stars (`DanmakusuStars.Rating`, `danmakusu_stars.csv` now md5, stars, version, bullets, beams, bosses; `StarRating.STORE_VERSION` 1 -> 2, so everything is rated again, and `StarRating.Result` has the counts). A map not rated yet is planned when song select shows it (on its own thread, 0.5-3.7 s on those maps), its bosses then without the song (kept only when the map did not need it); the background rating reads the song when the map needs it, as the boss menu does. (2) Sorts: Bullets, Beams and Most bosses after osu!'s in the sort dropdown (`SongSelectStars.BULLETS`/`BEAMS`/`BOSSES`, numbers 200-202 past osu!'s, kept in its settings as numbers; `Dropdown<SortMode>` patched to have them and name them, `FilterCriteria.RequiresSorting`, which throws for a sort it does not know, too). They go by what is kept of a map, in every mode; the sets stay together and go by their highest, as osu!'s BPM and length sorts do; a map not counted yet goes last. (3) Mods: a "Danmakusu" column (`Mods/DanmakusuModColumn`, crimson) before Difficulty Reduction in song select's mod screen (not in multiplayer's free mods) with this ruleset's own mods, the boss selections; Difficulty Reduction shows the rest, and its hotkeys are osu!'s again (the column has none). The mods stay Difficulty Reduction to osu! (`ModsColumnInSongSelect`, Harmony: the columns as they are made, and what each is given). `sectioncheck sortstars` (now with the sorts, the dropdown's names, the columns and what each gets) and new `sectioncheck counts <map.osu> [...]`. Not yet seen in osu!. If the ruleset is ever taken out while one of these sorts is chosen, osu! has a sort it does not know: choose another one first.
413. **(2026-11-08) Song select: the counts as bars, counted in the background; the sorts say "- Danmakusu" and list every difficulty on its own.** The user (with a screenshot: the counts were a small list, osu!'s difficulty bars beside them): place the Danmakusu stats like osu!'s, run their calculation in the background, add "- Danmakusu" to the sorts, and have them organize the songs like the sort by difficulty, without a set's panel opening into its difficulties. (1) osu! lays a map's counts out as bars all as wide as the widest name (at least 65) and shows them as a small list when they do not fit beside its difficulty bars (its own are three; four of ours, "Most bosses" among them, did not fit). Ours are laid out by `Stars/SongSelectCounts` (Harmony: `BeatmapTitleWedge.DifficultyStatisticsDisplay.updateLayout` skipped for ours, its `Update` lays them out): 65 wide where they fit, narrower down to each one's name and number where they do not, the list only when even that does not fit. osu!'s modes keep osu!'s layout. (2) A map not counted yet is no longer planned where song select asks (its panel kept the last map's numbers for 0.5-3.7 s): it is shown at once, with "…" for what is coming ("?" stays for a map that cannot be counted), and counted by `Stars/CountsInBackground` (one thread, below normal priority, the map chosen last first, 6 waiting at most, waits while a map is played); when it is done the panel shows it again (`SongSelectCounts.Refresh`, from `StarFiller.Update`). Bosses counted without the song are shown until osu! closes and not counted again. The tools still count where they ask (`sectioncheck counts`). (3) Sort names: "Bullets - Danmakusu", "Beams - Danmakusu", "Most bosses - Danmakusu" (they are in every mode's dropdown). (4) Our sorts never keep a set's difficulties together (`BeatmapCarouselFilterGrouping.ShouldGroupBeatmapsTogether` patched: false for them, as osu! has it for the sort by difficulty): every difficulty has a panel of its own, ordered by its own count (was: sets together, each going by its highest count), in every mode. `sectioncheck sortstars` prints whether the new patches took and "sets together: False" for our sorts. Not yet seen in osu!.
414. **(2026-11-08) The Danmakusu mods column is purple; no Spinners count.** The user (with a screenshot of the column: crimson header, Boss Selection on in Difficulty Reduction's green): colour purple; no need to count spinners, their bullets count as bullets (or not at all). (1) `DanmakusuModColumn.ACCENT` crimson (0.93, 0.27, 0.42) -> purple (0.61, 0.32, 0.88), a redder one than osu!'s Conversion column (#8c66ff). The column's mods are now `DanmakusuModPanel`s: when on, the panel and its badge (the hexagon and the acronym in it) are purple too, not green (the badge's private colours set before it shows them; if osu! renames them it stays green). The mods are still Difficulty Reduction to osu!, so elsewhere (the selected mods under song select, results) their icons stay green. (2) Song select's Spinners count is gone: Bullets, Beams, Most bosses. A spinner's stars were already among the bullets (the planner's spiral rings) and its beams among the beams, so the counts and the stars do not change.
415. **(2026-11-08) Danmakusu's mods are purple everywhere.** The user (screenshot: Triple Boss Selection on and purple, but its tooltip's "incompatible with" BS and DS icons green): why green. osu! colours a mod by its type wherever it draws one, and these are Difficulty Reduction to osu!; 414 recoloured only the panels of our column. New `Mods/PurpleMods` (Harmony): a mod of this ruleset is drawn in `DanmakusuModColumn.ACCENT` by osu!'s own drawables, `ModPanel` (its colour when on), `ModSwitchSmall` and `ModSwitchTiny` (the badges: on panels, the chosen mods, scores) and `ModIcon` (tooltips, the chosen mods under song select, results), set before they first show it. 414's `DanmakusuModPanel` is gone (the column has osu!'s panels again: the patch colours them). The mods stay Difficulty Reduction to osu!. `sectioncheck sortstars` prints whether it took. Not yet seen in osu!.
416. **(2026-11-08) On GitHub.** The user asked to post the ruleset to GitHub: only what builds the dll, and the dll. Public repo https://github.com/Begisso/Danmakusu (795 files: the code, the csproj, `Resources`; README with the user's disclaimer: a fan work of Touhou and other assets put together with Claude Opus 5.5 from their prompts, no license, no ownership claimed), release v2026.11.08 with the dll. The copy is `..\GitHub\Danmakusu` (`..\GitHub\sync.ps1` fills it; it builds on its own). The csproj's zip step now skips itself without `archive.ps1` (as on GitHub). git and the GitHub CLI were installed portable (MinGit 2.56, gh 2.103, checksums checked) in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs`, on the user's PATH: this Windows has no winget.
417. **(2026-11-12) Nemuno: the aiming beams stay parallel, twice as far apart.** When the copies have grown, the row of five turns as one round the middle beam until that one aims at the player (each turned round its own middle to aim at the player's place before, so they converged), flashes, and all five are thrown the same way, from the middle beam towards that place, side by side (`NemunoAct.beamAt`). `NEMUNO_COPY_GAP` 10 → 20 half widths between middles. ("Move parallel" read as both the aim and the throw; the other reading, parallel only while they turn, would keep them converging when thrown.)
418. **(2026-11-12) Engine pieces for the new bosses.** `StageBullet.Legs` (new `StageLeg`, `bouncingLegs`, `legPoint`): a stage bullet that flies straight legs worked out when it is made (stands at `P0` before the first), so an act's bullets can ricochet off the screen's edges as a closed form (Kana's, Yuki's stars); such a bullet is not traced by the others' ops, nor on the bullets' own clock. Knives with a yellow handle (`BulletInstance.YellowHandle`, `BulletEmitter.YellowHandle`, `StageBullet.YellowHandle`; the knife picture with its green turned yellow, `blueKnife(renderer, yellow: true)`). New planner list `own_shots_only`: bosses whose own shots are all their act's or their own rings, all through their section and in a spinner (no placeholder spirals, no spinner stars: added to `own_spinner_bullets` and `no_spinner_stars`).
419. **(2026-11-12) Misumaru Tamatsukuri (new mechanic `Orbs`, no longer a placeholder).** `OrbsAct`. All through her section (in a spinner too) she throws a yin-yang orb at the player every 8 beats (`MISUMARU_THROW_EVERY_BEATS`), and every 30th bullet (anyone's, counted ring by ring; `MISUMARU_ORB_EVERY`) turns into one where it is and flies on its way. Her orbs are Reimu's, 1.5 times as big (`MISUMARU_ORB_SIZE`), as fast, ricochet off the edges and off each other (simulated in 4 ms steps and kept, as Reimu's), hurt like a slider body, and shrink away after 16 beats (`REIMU_ORB_LIFE_BEATS`); at most 12 fly at once, the oldest shrinks away for a new one (`MISUMARU_ORBS_MOST`: added, "every 30th" makes dozens on a dense map). In her attacks bullets whose bodies touch ricochet off each other, once each (`Pushes`, a grid of the frame's bullets as Eika's): each that flies into the other is turned back off it as off a wall at its speed; the one seen first turns a frame later (`owed`); a flash where they touch. Not bullets younger than 300 ms (`MISUMARU_ARM_MS`), nor two of one ring. Out of her attacks the bullets are looked at only 60 ms every beat (for the orbs): every step cost 2.3 ms a frame next to Eirin's freezes. Her own spirals and the spinner's stars are gone. 3 stars.
420. **(2026-11-12) Suika Ibuki (new mechanic `Fissure`, was the placeholder `Density`).** `FissureAct`. All through her section, every 4 beats she stomps (`SUIKA_STOMP_*`): Hecatia's Earth hit; a ring goes out over the ground; what is within 4 circle radii of her (fired before the stomp, seen in the half beat after it) is thrown on its way twice as fast, orange-red, glowing (`TINT_FIRE`, bloom 0.6), with a beam for a tail 2 beats long that narrows to a point and hurts like a slider body (`SUIKA_TAIL_*`), and at the wall it bursts into 4 ellipses half its size, fanned over 90 degrees round the way it would bounce, at its old speed. The stomp hurts the player in it (150 ms), and 7 thin jagged cracks of molten rock round her (`SUIKA_CRACKS`, 0.15 circle radii wide) hurt like a slider body while they fade over 2 beats. Every 16 beats (`SUIKA_FISSURE_*`) a crack of molten rock, 2 circles across (2 circle radii on each side, uneven, narrower right at her), along the World's Ender's jagged profile, grows from her towards the player and out past the edge of the screen in a beat (Hecatia's cut sound), hurts like a slider body, and 8 beats after it has grown explodes (Hecatia's explosion sound, an orange flash): from 24 places along it, 4 ellipses to each side, the World's Ender's speeds, sizes, spread and glow; then it cools away. ("2 circles thick" read as two osu! circles across.) What she throws flies its own known way (`Untraced`): with Eirin's freezes every one was traced every frame (7.9 → 4.0 ms a frame for the two). Her basic attack is the stomp: no spirals, no spinner stars. 4 stars.
421. **(2026-11-12) Yuki (new mechanic `Scorch`, no longer a placeholder).** `ScorchAct`. In her attacks the laser bursts every boss had before the Master Spark: 5 beams round her (3 with another boss, 2 with two), the first aimed at the player as the attack begins, turning together as fast as a spinner's beams so the nearest comes round to the player, the slices flying out twice as fast as a plain bullet (`YUKI_BEAM_*`), 1.2 spinner-star radii wide on each side. Every slice goes out (fading) as far from her as the player was when it was fired, and every third leaves a scorch mark there (`YUKI_SCORCH_*`: charred, with flickering embers) that burns 8 beats and hurts like a slider body. Worked out in 30 ms steps and kept; a seek throws away what came after. Her own shots, all through her section (in a spinner too): every 4 beats, from 5 places 2.5 circle radii round her, a ring of 5 ricochet stars each (`YUKI_STAR*`), flying out from her, ricocheting once (`Legs`). 4 stars.
422. **(2026-11-12) Yumeko (new mechanic `Recall`, no longer a placeholder).** `RecallAct`; her fans are the planner's. A fan of 5 knives with yellow handles over 60 degrees at the player every 2 beats, all through her section, in a spinner too (`YUMEKO_FAN_*`). Every 8th bullet (anyone's but her knives, the first time it is seen on the screen; `YUMEKO_KNIFE_EVERY`) turns into a yellow-handled knife that stops and falls towards where the player was then, at four times Zanmu's pull (`YUMEKO_FALL_GRAVITY`). What goes all the way out of the screen is remembered in windows of 16 beats from her section's start (`YUMEKO_RECORD_BEATS`; at most 600) and comes back in the 4 beats after each window (`YUMEKO_RETURN_BEATS`; the last one after her section), in the order it left, four times as close together: a yellow-handled knife from where it left, straight across from that wall, at its speed. ("For 16 beats ... in 4 beats" read as back-to-back windows of her section, not tied to her attacks; the other reading: record in an attack, return at the start of the pause.) 4 stars.

A cross of beams grows out of her over a beat, past every edge (turned so the player is between two arms), 3 plain bullet radii wide on each side (the user: thrice as thick), glowing (bloom 0.6) and drawn as one piece so it has no seams (`LaserInstance.Piece`), with the big star a boss has while firing, Marisa's, on its middle over her (the user). It turns clockwise in her attacks and anticlockwise in her pauses, 1.5 times as fast as a spinner's beams (at least 20 degrees a second), hurts like a slider body, and shrinks back after her section. Every 4th bullet fired in her section (anyone's, counted ring by ring, as it is fired) comes out of the cross instead, the places spread evenly over the arms (golden-ratio steps, from 2.6 circle radii out), flying straight across the arm to one side or the other (half each) at its speed. Her own shots: every 4 beats a ring of 16 beams from her that wave a circle radius to each side as they fly (every second one the other way), 1.5 beats long, narrowing to their end, hurting like a slider body (`SHOU_*`). No spirals, no spinner stars. 4 stars.
424. **(2026-11-12) Eirin Yagokoro (new mechanic `Constellation`, was the placeholder `Elixir`).** `ConstellationAct` and BossOps. Every 8 beats from 4 beats into her section (`EIRIN_*`, `BossOps.ConstellationMarks`) every bullet on the screen stands still for 4 beats, a little lighter (`BossOps.Constellation`), and every 4 nearest each other are joined by a thin silver beam with a dark blue edge (her hair and dress), from one to the next nearest, that hurts like a slider body; then the beams are gone and everything flies on. They are joined a beat at a time (what is on the screen when the preview begins, then what is fired in each beat), each time in the order of the map's rings, so the same bullets make the same constellations; neighbours are looked for no farther than 5 cells of 48 px. Every 16 beats of her section 32 bullets on the screen (anyone's, fired before; the lowest of a hash of ring and bullet, the same on every play) stop where they are (Sakuya's stop sound); over 4 beats they grow to twice their size, hitbox too, turn white and glow; 8 beats after they stopped they fly off, each its own way by the hash, twice as fast as they flew, and ricochet off the edges 3 times (`Legs`; a flash at each) before they fly out (`KANA_*`). Her own shots, all through her section (in a spinner too: no spirals, no spinner stars): a ring of 8 butterflies every 4 beats (`LARVA_RING_*`; halved with another boss), the first at the player, every second ring turned half a step: the user's picture of a blue morpho, its white background taken away by new Bake mode `cutout` (`Resources/Textures/larva_butterfly.png`, 512 x 331, `StageTexture.Butterfly`, drawn in the bats' layer), facing the way it goes and flapping (its wings squeezed across, every 260 ms, `LARVA_FLAP_MS`), with a faint blue glow. They move as Wriggle's fireflies: 0.6 of a plain bullet's speed (`LARVA_SPEED`), wandering a three quarters of a circle radius to each side (`WRIGGLE_WANDER_*`); a spinner star's size, wings 3 radii across (`LARVA_WINGSPAN`). Every 8 beats (`LARVA_EVERY_BEATS`) every 20th bullet on the screen (the user corrected 40 to 20; anyone's, fired before, in the order of the map's rings; `LARVA_SWELL_EVERY`) is taken out of its ring and warned of for 4 beats as Patchouli's fire: it turns aquamarine (new `TINT_AQUAMARINE`), flies at half its speed (`LARVA_WARN_SLOW`) and has a red ring as big as it will grow, flashing faster and faster in the last two beats. Then, with Mokou's impact sound, it swells in 150 ms to a round bullet 2 circle radii across (`LARVA_SWELL_CIRCLES`; drawn as sharp as Mokou's ball, hitbox too, glowing 0.7) at its own speed, shrinks back over the last beat of the next four, and flies on as it was, still aquamarine. ("Swells ... then return back" read as swollen for the four beats after the warning, shrinking back in the last.) Headless (Chirality [Asymmetry] 3:00 + 15 s): 1.05 ms a frame alone, 2.5 with Eirin. 3 stars.
430. **(2026-11-13) The example maps are no longer in Downloads.** `..\Tools\SectionCheck\maps.ps1` still points at `%USERPROFILE%\Downloads\<set>` folders that are gone; the .osu files are in `H:\osu maps\osu files` (the benches above used that copy). maps.ps1 not changed yet.
431. **(2026-11-13) New mod: Always Boss (`AB`, `DanmakusuModAlwaysBoss`).** The user: boss sections during the entire song, breaks and spinners staying as they are. The mod marks the converted map (`SectionMarks`, `IApplicableAfterBeatmapConversion`) and `BulletPlanner.Sections` then takes `alwaysBossSections`: the map's breaks (as `mapSections` gives them a boss) and every spinner (one in a kiai too) as their own sections, and every stretch between them, from the first object to the last, a section of the kiai kind (a break before it starts the kiai beam count again); the song's intense parts are not looked for. What is too short goes on with its neighbour as ever (`continued`). Without a boss selection mod there are still no bosses. Chirality [Asymmetry]: 164 s of boss -> 208 s (all of it from the first object at 0:37 to 4:05). The difficulty calculator still keeps its rating for song select with it on (it changes no stars). In the purple Danmakusu column (it is ours and of the Difficulty Reduction type).
432. **(2026-11-13) Boss picker: attacks and pauses per boss.** The user: a slider for the least number of attacks and pauses each boss gets, which changes how many bosses fit. A second slider under the boss count (1..8, `BossPicker.MOST_ATTACKS`): `MostBosses(map, bands, attacks)` counts room for that many attack-and-pause pieces a boss (a spinner holds one boss only when it is that long), the bosses slider's top end and Max follow it, and a count above it comes down. Kept in the picker's value as `count;attacks|ids` (`ValueOf`, `AttacksOf`; a value saved before has no `;attacks` and means 1). The bar preview reads the chosen mods (`IBindable<IReadOnlyList<Mod>>`): with Always Boss or Map Generation on, the map's sections and room are theirs. Chirality [Asymmetry]: 17/6/3/2 bosses fit with 1/2/3/4 attacks each (Always Boss: 20/8/5/3). The planner shares the sections out as before; the slider bounds the count.
433. **(2026-11-13) New mod: Map Generation (`MG`, `DanmakusuModMapGeneration`), a generator trained on the downloaded maps.** The user: a mod that ignores the map and plays a generated one, osu!standard maps only, a Stars slider 1..10 (default 6); then "train map generator according to osu map sample we downloaded before" and "map generator should be aware of song audio as well". (1) **The model** (`Generation/MapModel.cs`, embedded `Resources/Generation/map_model.json`, 2.4 MB): new SectionCheck mode `trainmap` read all 10,037 osu!standard maps of `H:\osu maps\osu files` that have Danmakusu stars in `danmakusu_stars.csv` (226 s on every core) and counted, for each whole number of Danmakusu stars 1..10 (165 to 2282 maps a bucket): the median CS, AR, OD, HP, slider multiplier and hitsound volume; the rhythm as tokens (twelfths of a beat since the object before, circle / slider / spinner, repeats, length), how often each comes after none, one and two others (pruned: counts under 2 or 3 and thin contexts dropped); jump distances (12 px bins) and turn angles (15 degree bins) by how close in time objects are (a quarter beat, a half, a beat, more), how often a turn goes the same way round; how often sliders curve; whistle, clap and finish on the first beat of a bar, on a beat, between beats; combo lengths. Rhythms are counted in the bosses' beat (`MapModel.BeatAt` = `BossBeat(CapBeat)`: a 300 BPM song mapped in halves is the rhythm of a 150 BPM one in quarters; counted in raw beats one song came out 30-43 stars). (2) **The generator** (`Generation/MapGenerator.cs`): the map's own objects are thrown away; its timing, breaks (nothing put in them) and length stay. Objects follow each other as the model's rhythm says (the two before; backing off to one, none), on the beat grid; each jumps from the cursor as far and turns as sharply as the model has for that rhythm, inside the playfield (12 tries, then bounced in); sliders straight or curved (a quadratic Bezier whose length fits), as long as their time needs at the model's slider multiplier (velocity 1, by the timing point's real beat), and when none fits a line that bounces off the playfield's edges; hitsounds by where in the bar; combos as the model's. Deterministic (xorshift seeded by artist, title, difficulty name and stars). (3) **The song**: the Map Generation mod hears it through new Harmony patch `PlayableBeatmapSource` (round `WorkingBeatmap.GetPlayableBeatmap`: the working beatmap being made playable on that thread; `SongBands.From` its waveform, as the sections already read it; without it the generator works without the song). Of 6 next objects the model offers, the one that lands on a drum hit (a kick or snare within 35 ms, new `SongBands.Hits`, `HitNear`) scores higher, dense ones where the song is loud and sparse ones where it is quiet (loudness ranked over the song, two beats round each beat: the model's density stays on the whole), with a little chance; jumps 0.8-1.2 times as long from quiet to loud; finishes twice as often on a strong hit. (4) **Stars**: new mode `gentest calibrate` generated 30 songs (15 with their audio taken out of their .osz) at 13 sources (the 10 buckets mixed between neighbours, and three past the last that draw its rhythm closer together, 0.3 a step) and rated them as the game does; the medians (1.49, 2.17, 3.05, 4.07, 4.60, 4.76, 5.46, 5.91, 5.89, 7.65, 8.65, 9.93, 10.38) are the model's `MeanStars`, and every level's source is where they cross it (`SourceFor`: 1, 1.74, 2.94, 3.93, 6.34, 9.05, 9.62, 10.35, 11.27, 12.16). In the game the generated map is then rated (with osu!'s processor, which marks the last object of each combo - a ring of stars here; without it the ratings were a star low and the maps a star too hard) and made again up to twice from a corrected source (the song's factor against the average, then the line through the last two), the nearest kept: `gentest check` on 40 other songs, medians per level 1.53, 1.96, 2.93, 4.00, 5.00, 5.95, 6.94, 7.98, 8.91, 9.91; within half a star 50, 95, 80, 90, 90, 82, 68, 53, 65, 50 %. It costs 0.8-4.3 s when the map is made (with the mod only). The ratings song select keeps are not written with it on. (5) New mode `genmap <map.osu> <stars> <out.osu> [<audio>]` writes the generated map as an osu! file. Chirality [Asymmetry] with its song: 3* -> 603 objects (3.18 stars), 6* -> 1197 (4.90), 9* -> 2062 (8.72); the field with bosses on the 6* runs at 0.6-1.1 ms a frame. ("Mapless songs": osu!lazer has no songs without a map in its library, so song select has none to show; not done. The way there would be a tool that turns audio files into empty maps and imports them.)
434. **(2026-11-13) CLAUDE.md and SKILLS.md: the generator, its tool modes and how to retrain it.**
435. **(2026-11-13) Map Generation: every mode, and the maps stay as they were.** The user: "let map generation completely replace other mode maps too", "just treat them as osu standard maps", then "make sure map generation does not kill maps, after turning off maps should not mess up and return to normal". (1) **Every mode**: the mod no longer skips mania, taiko and catch maps; what it makes of them is a plain osu!standard map (circles, sliders, spinners, stack leniency 0.7, none of the converted modes' rings or falling). The Stars slider is unchanged (1..10, whole stars, default 6). (2) **The bug that broke maps**: osu! hands the map to the mod before its objects are made ready, when a slider's end is not known and seems endless, so the generator took the map for endless and filled it to its 20,000-object cap (FELT - COLORS [Hard] at 4*: 20,000 objects reaching hours past the song, 69 s): osu! gives up on a map it cannot make ready within 10 s ("Timed out while loading beatmap"), so the map could not be played or shown with the mod on. Now the generator makes the objects ready first (`MapGenerator.spanOf`): 687 objects, 3:36 like the song, 0.5 s. (3) **Within osu!'s time**: the tries that correct the stars rate 4,000 objects at most in all (`rate_objects_budget`, counted in objects, not on the clock, so every computer makes the same map and replays stay right); a bigger map keeps the calibrated source unrated. The source the tries settled on is remembered for the map and stars (`settled`), so song select's panel, stars and boss menu and then the play make it again at once (the same map, 5-840 ms instead of 0.1-3.9 s). The most objects grows with the song (25 a second, at least 20,000): a 27-minute marathon at 10* was cut at 20:53, now filled to its end (23,613 objects, 1.1 s). (4) **Nothing kept from a changed map**: song select counted a generated map's bullets, beams and bosses and kept them under the real map's MD5 when that map had not been counted yet (the map then showed the generated map's numbers with the mod off; and Always Boss's boss count likewise). A map a Danmakusu mod changed is marked (`SectionMarks.MarkGenerated`, `ChangedBy`: "MG6", "AB", "MG6+AB"), counted on its own under a name that is never kept (`CountsInBackground.Request(md5, map, changedBy)`, `DanmakusuBeatmap.Count(..., keep: false)`) and shown only while the mod is on; the difficulty calculator does not keep a generated map's stars either (it already did not with the mod). osu! itself converts a copy of the map for every play (the map, its file, its difficulty and its timing are never touched; checked). New SectionCheck mode `mgsafe <map.osu> [...]` (`MGSAFE_STARS="1,6,10"` for the levels, `MGSAFE_NOLIMIT=1` without osu!'s 10 s): one map of each mode (FELT - COLORS, Body F10ating, Neverending streams, Knight Rider mania, Hitech pistol taiko, a catch map), 1/6/10 stars: every load 0.1-3.9 s, the same map the second time, the map without the mod the same before and after, the map osu! keeps unchanged, nothing kept.
436. **(2026-11-13) Autoplay reworked: the character dodges by itself, as little as it can.** The user: "Player moves as less as possible and performs freeze mechanics to avoid undodgable obstacles, but everything just enough to finish map hitless", "for wasd control too". osu!'s Autoplay is Danmakusu's own in this ruleset (`Mods/DanmakusuModAutoplay.cs`, same name and acronym, also inside the Autoplay/Cinema pair; `DanmakusuRuleset.withOwnAutoplay`): its replay is one still cursor in the middle, and the field moves the character (`UI/BulletField.AutoPilot.cs`, on whenever a `ModAutoplay` is on, never in the boss menu's preview). It is the star rating's lazy player (`ForcedMovement`) on the real field: it looks `AUTO_HORIZON_MS` 400 ahead in steps of `AUTO_SAMPLE_MS` 25 and stands still while nothing would touch it; otherwise it walks the shortest way (`AUTO_WAYS` 16 ways, `AUTO_LENGTHS` 6 lengths from `AUTO_SMALLEST_DODGE` 8 px to as far as it gets) to a place it can stay at safely all that time, the nearest the middle of the screen of those as near; with keyboard controls at the keyboard's speed (the player's own), with the mouse at `AUTO_CURSOR_SPEED` 0.6 px/ms; if no walk is safe it teleports (the freeze keys' teleport, or Space: not swept) to the nearest place that is (a grid every `AUTO_GRID` 16 px, `AUTO_EDGE` 12 px in from the screen's edges, places nothing passes at all first); nowhere safe: where it is touched latest. It keeps `AUTO_MARGIN` 3 px round its 1 px hitbox. What it foresees: every ring's bullets on their own clocks (rings fired within the time too; homing ones straight on, with 14 px more room), the stages' bullets (one the ops steer straight on from where the hit check last saw it), beams (on a grid of moments every 100 ms, kept from frame to frame, wider by half a step's flight), the moon's stars (every 50 ms), circles about to be hit, slider bodies, and every act's `Hurts` asked for the place and moment; new `StageAct.Springs` for what goes off once (Flandre's eye explosions, Enoko's armed traps). The skinned cursor is drawn on the character while it plays. Speed: a ring that is far away, or empty (all its bullets burst, used up or taken: Cirno's freezes keep thousands of them), is passed by from the hit check's snapshot or its spread and remembered for 60 ms. Misumaru's orbs went on where they stood when asked about a moment not simulated yet: now straight on from their last step (`OrbsAct.positionOf`). Shou's waving beams were worked out whole for every question: now only a beam whose band and swing the place is in (`PagodaAct.Hurts`, the game's own check gets faster too). New bench options (SectionCheck `field`): `AUTO_PILOT=1` (`AUTO_KEYS=1` with keyboard controls) and `BENCH_STILL=1` (standing still), both counting the hits by cause, and what the autoplay costs. Chirality [Asymmetry] 0:48 + 20 s, standing still against autoplay: Nue 184 -> 0, Toyohime 307 -> 0, Junko 236 -> 0, Eirin 200 -> 0, Megumu 211 -> 0 (after 438), Shou 160 -> 0, Kana 190 -> 0, Yuki 197 -> 0, Misumaru 154 -> 0, Eternity Larva 179 -> 0, Utsuho 177 -> 0, Marisa 209 -> 0, Yukari 284 -> 0, Clownpiece 166 -> 0, Raiko 173 -> 0, Hecatia 153 -> 3 (thrown worlds), Kagerou 1234 -> 0, Youmu 181 -> 0, Patchouli 893 -> 0, Flandre 97 -> 0, Cirno + Sakuya 135 -> 1-2, Suika + Yumeko 298 -> 0; with keyboard controls Nue, Junko, Utsuho, Marisa 0. It costs 1-4 ms a frame on top of the field (Cirno + Sakuya, the heaviest, 3.8).
437. **(2026-11-13) Mod screen: a mod's settings panel over the Danmakusu column.** The user: "Mod advanced Settings should be Above Danmakusu Column instead" (the "Mod Customisation" panel osu! puts at the top right). `DanmakusuModColumn.placeCustomisation` moves osu!'s panel (`ModSelectOverlay.customisationPanel`, found by reflection) over the Danmakusu column every frame: its left edge on the column's, right of the search box when they would overlap, kept on the screen, following the column as the columns scroll; back where osu! has it in a ruleset where the column has nothing. If osu! renames the panel it stays where osu! has it (logged once).
438. **(2026-11-13) Megumu: static beams, bent like a galaxy's arms.** The user: "her laserbeams are static, but angle of next laserbeam set is turned 5 degres relative to previous. Also laserbeams itself are bend 20 degrees relative to line that connects megumu and laserbeam shooting point". A set no longer moves with her (it did: she stands opposite the player, so the beams swung with every move of the player): it stays where she was when its preview came (`GalaxyAct` anchors, taken again when time goes back). Each beam starts `MEGUMU_SHOOT_CIRCLES` 1.5 circle radii from there on its own way out and goes `MEGUMU_BEND_DEGREES` 20 degrees off that way, all the same way round (a pinwheel). Each set is still turned 5 degrees from the one before (the other way round every 32 beats). Description updated.
439. **(2026-11-13) Map Generation: maps with the structure of mapped ones, and the map's own hitsounds.** The user: "follow trained maps structures as well, they look like a mess, also hitsounds". Every jump's length and turn and every hitsound were drawn on their own, object by object. Now (`MapGenerator`): (1) **rhythm by bars**: at a new bar the rhythm of the bar before is played again (its tokens, from its first object's kind on) with a chance of 0.6, 0.3 after it has been once (`bar_repeat_chance`, `bar_repeat_again_chance`); (2) **combos** start on a new bar once they have 3 objects, or end at a length drawn from the model (was the model's length alone); (3) **a shape per combo** (`Pattern`): every jump of the same rhythm in a combo is as long (drawn once per rhythm per combo from the model's, give or take `spacing_jitter` 8 %), and turns by the same angle, the same way round (polygons, curved streams) or zigzagging, as often as the model's maps turn the same way; the rhythm counted from the end of the object before, as mappers space. When the shape leaves the playfield the turn is tried the other way round and 15-45 degrees off, then a jump as long towards the middle (was: any turn, then bounced off the edges, which changed the length); (4) **hitsounds**: an object takes the sounds of the map's own object at its moment (within an eighth of a beat, 40 ms at most: the head, every repeat and the tail of a slider on their own; `OwnSounds`), with their sampleset and volume; where the map has none, the sampleset and volume of the nearest and the model's additions on fixed places (a clap on the backbeat, a whistle on the bar's first beat, a finish on the first beat of every fourth bar and on a strong drum hit, each where the model's maps have it often enough) instead of drawn at random. FELT - COLORS [Hard] (the map: 4.0 objects a combo, the same rhythm in a combo spaced 7 % apart, additions on 18 %): at 3* 3.5 a combo, 4 %, 28 %; at 6* 3.9, 4 %, 18 % (before: 4.6-6.7 a combo, 25-37 % apart). Recalibrated (`gentest calibrate`, 390 maps; `SourceFor` 1, 1.41, 2.83, 3.87, 5.84, 9.29, 9.76, 11.33, 12.02, 13.00); `gentest check` on 40 songs, medians per level 1.95, 2.08, 2.98, 4.00, 5.04, 5.82, 7.24, 8.62, 9.19, 9.78 (level 1 is a star too hard: even the easiest bucket comes out at about 1.8 now).
440. **(2026-11-13) Songs without maps: a placeholder each, shown with Map Generation; the song's timing fixed.** The user: "When Map Generation is choosen - all mapless songs get one emptly placeholder map", "songs without maps at all, just songs in folder", the folder `%APPDATA%\osu\danmakusu songs` (made). (1) **Placeholders** (`Generation/Placeholders.cs`): every song file there (mp3, ogg, wav, flac, m4a) gets an .osz of its own - the song and one empty osu!standard map, "Artist - Title" from the file's name, creator Danmakusu, difficulty "Map Generation", tag `danmakusu-placeholder`, one timing point from the song's tempo - imported into osu! by the background rating (`StarFiller`) when osu! starts and every two minutes, once per file (`danmakusu_songs.txt` in osu!'s data folder: a placeholder deleted in osu! is not made again; a changed file is). Song select shows a placeholder only in Danmakusu with Map Generation on (`SongSelectStars.matchPrefix`; it filters again when the mod is turned on or off, `Refilter`), never in osu!'s own modes. The generator fills a map with no objects from the first beat at the song's first drum hit to a beat before its end. (2) **Tempo** (`Generation/Tempo.cs`): how well the drum hits line up on a beat grid of every length from 60 to 300 BPM (weighted towards 170 BPM), to a hundredth of a ms near the best; the beats where the most kicks fall (`SongBands.Kicks`), the bar's first beat where the hits are hardest. New SectionCheck mode `tempotest` against 21 mapped songs: the tempo right on 12 (to 0.01 BPM), none doubled or halved; the beats on 2 of those within 20 ms of the mapped ones, the others up to 180 ms off (the grid the drums suggest is not always where the mapper put the beat). Mode `placeholder <song> <out.osz> [stars]` makes one and fills it. (3) **The song's timing** (`SongBands`): osu!'s waveform has (int)(frequency x 0.001 x channels) samples per point, so a 44.1 kHz song's points are 0.9977 ms, not 1: every time read from it was late by 0.23 % (0.4 s three minutes in: the drum hits the generator listens to, the sections chosen by the song). Now the frequency is read from the song file's header (`MsPerPoint`, `FromFile`) and everything is in real ms: tempos found came out 0.23 % slow before, exact after. And the hits were found a median 16 ms after the objects of the mapped songs (the waveform's bands are over 23 ms blocks): `hit_lag_ms` 16 is taken off.
441. **(2026-11-13) Mod settings panel wider; no boss menu when bosses are checked; Shou's cross half as fast.** The user: "Make mod settings fall menu wider so boss names can fit", "if you select at least one boss here no need to show boss selection menu when you already started the game", "Shou - halve spin speed". (1) Over the Danmakusu column osu!'s settings panel is 760 wide (osu!'s 400: four boss tiles of the picker's own 168 and the room round them; never wider than the screen; back to osu!'s width where the column has nothing). (2) The menu before a map with a boss selection mod (`BossMenu`) is not shown when at least one boss is checked in the mod's settings: the map starts at once with them (`DanmakusuModBossSelection.ApplyToHUD`); how the player moves stays as last chosen. (3) Shou: `SHOU_CROSS_SPIN` 1.5 -> 0.75 (times a spinner's beams), `SHOU_CROSS_LEAST_DEGREES` 20 -> 10 (degrees a second at least); description "faster than" -> "a little slower than a spinner's beams"; difficulty 4 -> 3 stars.
442. **(2026-11-13) Autoplay: no more freezes.** The user: "Auto is also too heavy it freezes the game". (1) osu! plays a replay in even steps and, after a heavy frame, runs step after step to catch up: Autoplay's replay is only a still cursor, so it now goes in real steps as a play does (`DrawableDanmakusuRuleset`: frame-stable playback only for a real replay; the clock is unstuck in Autoplay too). (2) A budget per frame, `AUTO_BUDGET_MS` 8: past it the autoplay takes the best way found so far. Walks of up to `AUTO_SHORT_WALK` 64 px are looked at first with only what comes near them gathered (most dodges are short), the longer ones while there is time; a teleport near it first (`AUTO_TELEPORT_NEAR` 260 px), on the whole screen only with time left (200 places looked at closely at most, was 400). Chirality [Asymmetry] 0:48 + 20 s: worst frames 13-70 ms (were up to 280); hits Flandre 2, Youmu 0, Toyohime 1, Clownpiece 0, Shou 0, Cirno + Sakuya 4, Suika + Yumeko 0, Nue 0, Hecatia 0; three bosses: Nue + Junko + Utsuho 0, Marisa + Youmu + Flandre 16; Megumu (alone 132, with Cirno and Sakuya 117) is too costly to look ahead for within the budget: her stars make every bullet ricochet.
443. **(2026-11-13) Map Generation: the structure learnt from the maps, 1 star reachable, kiai busier and intros calmer, a tempo in a song's file name.** The user: "follow all written advices". (1) **Learnt** (`trainmap`, new `MapModel.Bucket` fields, the generator's hand-set values before as their defaults): in a combo, how far apart the jumps of the same rhythm are (`SpacingJitter`: 1* 10 %, 3* 19 %, 5* 32 %, 7* 42 %, 9-10* 47-49 %; by hand 8 %), how much its turns differ (`TurnSpread`, about 31 degrees at every level; by hand none), how many combos zigzag (`Zigzag` 20-26 %; by hand from the turns' sides), how often a bar has the rhythm of the bar before (`BarRepeat` 5-16 %, and 33-54 % after one already had: by hand 60 % and 30 %), how often an object on a bar's first beat starts a combo (`ComboOnDownbeat` 52 % at 1* to 84 % at 8*: by hand always when the combo had 3), how far curved sliders bend from the line between their ends (`Bend`, a histogram: by hand 15-40 %). (2) **1 star**: below the first source the rhythm is drawn further apart (`stretch_per_step` 1: at source 0.25 the gaps are 1.75 times as long), and `MapModel.SourceOf`/`Expected` go on below source 1 along the first step (down to 1 - `BELOW` 0.75); calibrated with 16 sources (was 13) so that the top levels are measured, not guessed. (3) **Kiai and intro** (`Intensity`): the busyness the generator chooses by is at least 0.8 in the map's kiai and at most 0.3 in its intro (to the first kiai; without one, to where the song first gets loud, a third of the map at most, or 15 % of it without the song), also without the song. FELT - COLORS [Hard] at 5*: 3.5 objects a second in the intro, 6.2 in kiai, 5.6 elsewhere. (4) **A song without a map** may say its tempo in its file name: `Artist - Title [180].mp3`, `[180 BPM]`, or with where a bar starts `[180 1234]` (`Placeholders.Make`; the beats placed by the drums when only the tempo is given: `Tempo.Place`). Retrained on the 10,037 maps; calibrated (`SourceFor` 0.25, 1.37, 2.75, 3.95, 5.98, 9.43, 10.99, 11.80, 13.27, 13.94); `gentest check` on 40 songs, medians per level 1.34, 1.99, 3.05, 4.06, 4.93, 6.00, 7.44, 8.21, 9.13, 10.36 (were 1.95, 2.08, 2.98, 4.00, 5.04, 5.82, 7.24, 8.62, 9.19, 9.78). `mgsafe`: every load within osu!'s 10 s (3.7 s at most), the maps as they were. `genmap` also shows how busy the intro, the kiai and the rest are.
444. **(2026-11-13) Autoplay: what it foresees is kept from frame to frame.** The user: "follow all written advices" (the recommendation: keep each bullet's foreseen way instead of working out the next 0.4 s of every bullet again every frame), then "no autoplay quality setting, choose one that fits for your algorithm" (a setting "Thinking per frame" was made and taken out again: `AUTO_BUDGET_MS` stays 8 ms). The samples are this frame's moment and then the moments of a grid every `AUTO_SAMPLE_MS` (the same moments frame after frame: `autoSetTimes`). For every ring that has been fired and is not homing, where each bullet is at each grid moment is worked out once and kept (`RingTrack`: its clock and its bullets per moment, a ring buffer of `auto_samples` + 3 moments), so a frame works out only the newest moment; this frame's own moment is between the two round it. A ring whose bullet is turned at runtime (a deflection) is worked out again, a moment kept longer than 400 ms too, and every ring when time goes back; rings not looked at for 200 ms are forgotten. Beams' shapes are kept 300 ms (`auto_beam_keep_ms`: one cut or blown up meanwhile is only dodged when it need not be); stage bullets are worked out at every second moment (halfway between, by time). Chirality [Asymmetry] 0:48 + 20 s, 8 ms: Megumu 132 -> 1 hit at 6.0 ms a frame (was 12), Cirno + Sakuya 1, Youmu 0, Toyohime 0; three at a time: Nue + Junko + Utsuho 0 (10.7 ms a frame), Marisa + Youmu + Flandre 3 (14.8 ms, was 33), Megumu + Cirno + Sakuya 72 (was 117: every bullet of the three steered by them, the heaviest there is).

446. **(2026-11-13) Triple bosses and the autoplay: faster, and the autoplay no longer starves.** The user: "what can be done to optimize triple bosses", "follow advices". The log (Autoplay, Triple Boss Selection with 90 bosses, Camellia - Looking for Edge of Ground [ARTIFICIAL REALITY]) had Always Boss on (found by the bench: with `ALWAYS_BOSS=1` its live rings drop at 0:38 and 0:49 as the log's did): Byakuren / Junko / Wakasagihime were on from 0:41.5 to 1:06.2, the autoplay took up to 41 ms a frame there (15 frames a second), and the replay failed at 1:06.6 (the jump to 2,964 rings in that frame is the fail: every ring is looked at again so that it can fall). Entry 445's "about 1:20" was wrong. Changes:
    - **Wakasagihime** (`TideAct.Hurts`): a box round every trace, worked out once a frame, and a side test per blade before its points (it compared every segment of 300-400 traces for every place the autoplay looked at).
    - **Kept moments** (new `MomentShapes`, BulletField.Stage.cs): an act asked about the same moment a second time works out its shapes once, keeps them until its next `Update` (runs of points with boxes, a grid of 48 px squares over them) and answers from them; the first question is answered as before, so the game's own hit check is unchanged. Used by Ringo's skewers (`RingoAct`), Sekibanki's and Byakuren's beams (`HeadAct`) and Satori's trail.
    - **Byakuren** (`HeadAct`): a beam whose slices are gone after their time is skipped when the player is further from the head than it has flown since. The autoplay sees a head that still flies go on straight (`ahead`), with its beam behind it, for moments past the next step (it saw heads stand still).
    - **The autoplay's budget** (`AUTO_BUDGET_MS` 8, unchanged) now starts when the search for a way out starts (it started at the frame's start: when gathering what is near and asking whether it can stay took it all, only the shortest walks were looked at, and it was hit); a long walk stops looking within its length once the time is up (every 4 ways); the nearest 32 teleport places are always looked at (new `auto_teleport_least`). A ring passed by stays passed by for 2.5 frames, 60 to 150 ms (was 60 ms: at 15 fps every ring was looked at again every frame).
    - **Beams worked out once a frame**: the hit check keeps the points `GetPoints` gave (`beamPoints`, `keptBeams`), and drawing at the same moment takes them (they were worked out twice; forgotten every frame, on a resize and when a beam is made to last longer).
    - **Sorting the live rings**: more than 64 added since the last sort (a seek, a resize, the fail) is a whole sort (`Array.Sort`, same order); the insertion sort took 40 ms with 2,000 rings.
    - Measured (`field`, 0:56 + 15 s, with the autoplay; ms a frame, old → new): Eiki 16.6 → 9.5, Shion 9.8 → 6.8, Youmu 8.8 → 3.9, Zanmu 7.6 → 5.5, Okina 6.4 → 3.1, Ringo 19.9 → 11.1, Sekibanki 10.9 → 4.4, Wakasagihime 13.6 (worst 240) → 6.1 (worst 30), Junko + Wakasagihime 34.4 (worst 458) → 8.2 (worst 106); "nowhere safe" frames 130 / 137 / 8 → 0. The logged game with Always Boss, 40 ms frames and a starved budget: 51 hits, health down to 0.23 → 19 hits, at least 0.83; without the autoplay (0:00-2:00, no Always Boss) 8.2 → 7.0 ms a frame.
    - Bench (`field`): `HIT_TIMES=1`, `BENCH_FRAME_MS`, `AUTO_BUDGET`, `RING_COUNTS=1` (live rings every second, as the log's slow-second lines), `ALWAYS_BOSS=1` (also for `periods`).
