## Nue (UFOs)

Code: `UI/Stages/UfoAct.cs`, `UI/BulletField.Stage.cs` (`buildStage`, `nextUfoAttack`), `Bullets/BulletPlanner.cs`, `DanmakusuBalance.cs` (`NUE_*`), `Bosses/BossTuning.cs` (`nue_*`).

### 2026-10-18

1. **Pull phase is never cut short.** A section switch or the end of the map no longer ends her attack early: `BulletPlanner` stops clipping Nue's attack ends to the section end. Other bosses are unchanged. In a spinner section the spinner is her single attack and still ends with it.
2. **Only some bullets are pulled.** First every 3rd, then changed to every 2nd bullet of a ring (`NUE_PULL_EVERY = 2`). Chosen by bullet index, so replays and seeks are the same.
3. **Pull force tripled.** `nue_pull` default 0.3125 → 0.9375.
4. **Slower shooting.** Release frequency halved, then shoot duration doubled again: `NUE_SHOOT_RATE` 2 → 1 → 0.5. Bullet speed unchanged.
5. **Shoot phase capped.** It ends a quarter beat before Nue's next attack, also when that attack is in a later section. After her final attack it runs its full length.
6. **Every pulled bullet is shot out.** A bullet that can't reach its UFO before the pull ends isn't pulled (it flies on). The 600-bullets-per-UFO limit was removed.
7. **Pull layout.** The 8 UFOs are spread evenly round the walls (first at the middle of the top wall, then clockwise), aiming at the centre. Replaces the corner pinwheel.
8. **Cones twice as visible.** `NUE_CONE_ALPHA` 0.25 → 0.5.
9. **Cones warn earlier.** First drawn at fixed spots 2 beats before launch; then changed so the cones ride the UFOs from launch. They're dimmer while the UFOs fly to their places (`NUE_CONE_WARN_SHARE = 0.4`) and full brightness once pulling or shooting starts. Shoot cones fade in while the UFOs gather in the middle.
10. **UFOs slide during the pull.** They move round the walls in the same direction the shoot-phase circle turns (one lap per turn of the circle). Pulled bullets aim at where their UFO will be when they arrive.
11. **Turn direction alternates.** Both phases of a pull-and-shoot sequence turn the same way; the next sequence turns the other way. Counted over the whole map per boss slot, so it doesn't reset at a section change.
12. **Pull everything again.** Every bullet in a cone is pulled (`NUE_PULL_EVERY` removed), except bullets the UFOs already shot out — those are stage bullets, which the pull never looks at.
13. **Pull force doubled.** `nue_pull` default 0.9375 → 1.875.
14. **Rotation halved.** `NUE_TURN_SHARE` 0.25 → 0.125: both the slide round the walls and the turning circle in the shoot phase.
15. **Every-second-bullet rule restored.** Rolled back change 12: only every 2nd bullet of a ring is pulled again (`NUE_PULL_EVERY = 2`). Shot-out bullets are still never pulled.

## Toyohime (free fall, pull towards the player)

### 2026-10-18

1. **Pull halved.** `toyohime_turn` default 0.00075 → 0.000375.
2. **Golden magic circle.** The thin ring round the player is now a golden magic circle (thick outer ring at the edge of the pull, a band of rune marks, a thin inner ring, a faint eight-pointed star) that turns slowly (`TOYOHIME_CIRCLE_*`).
3. **Arrows towards the player.** 8 golden chevrons on the circle keep sliding in towards the player, one after another, each over 2 beats (`TOYOHIME_ARROW*`).
4. **Arrows close to the player; pulled bullets golden.** The arrows now slide from 2.2 to 1.1 circle radii from the player (instead of along the circle's edge) and are smaller (0.9 → 0.6). Every bullet she pulls is marked from the moment the pull turns it (per-bullet `GoldMask`, cleared on rewind) and drawn gold (`TINT_YELLOW`, 85%) with bloom 0.8 (`TOYOHIME_GOLD_*`).
5. **Weaker, every second bullet.** Pull halved again: `toyohime_turn` default 0.000375 → 0.0001875 (slider minimum lowered to 0.00005 so the new default fits). Only every second bullet of a ring is pulled (`TOYOHIME_PULL_EVERY` = 2, by bullet index); the others fly on and stay their own colour. Dropping at the top of the screen still applies to every bullet.

## Junko

### 2026-10-18

1. **Recorded bullets are reshot.** Every 16 beats of her section, every bullet that appeared in those beats (anyone's) is counted, and she shoots that many out again as one ring from where she is, at their average speed (weighted per bullet). Her own reshot rings aren't counted, so the rings don't snowball. A partial window at the end of the section doesn't fire. Rings over 64 bullets are split into several (`MAX_RING_BULLETS`).

## Byakuren (mechanic `Scrolls`, no longer a placeholder)

### 2026-10-18

1. **Remembered spots.** She records where every 5th circle/slider of her section starts. 32 beats later a beam fires from each spot straight at the player (aimed when it starts, never turns), half as wide as Marisa's widest beam, constant width, for 8 beats. Shots are at least 2 beats apart (a spot waits for the one before it), so a dense map fires every 2 beats: 0, 2, 4, 6, 8, ... Shots still fire after her section ends. (`BYAKUREN_RECORD_EVERY`, `_DELAY_BEATS`, `_SHOT_GAP_BEATS`, `_BEAM_BEATS`, `_SPOT_BEAM_SIZE`)
2. **Big star on each spot.** While a spot fires, the boss's big star (as under Marisa) sits on it in Byakuren's colours (lightened purple star, peach glow), spinning (`SpotStarAct`).
3. **Her own beams.** Her attack bursts are 5 slider-width beams from a pentagon of points round her (`BYAKUREN_POINT_RADIUS` 40 px). Each starts pointing straight away from the player and is steered round towards the player, half a turn in 2 beats (`BYAKUREN_HOMING_BEATS`), so the beams curl in.
4. **Spinner.** Her spinner gets the same 5 beams, pointing straight out from her and spinning at the spinner beams' speed instead of homing.
5. **Shake.** All of these are turning beams, which already shake the screen 1–2 px while they fire.

### 2026-10-18 (fix)

6. **Her homing beams were never made.** The burst loop skips beam creation for every boss but Marisa (and Reisen); Byakuren is now let through too.
7. **Clones count.** When Doremy or Sagume shares a section with her, their copied circles and sliders are recorded too (every 5th of originals and copies together, in time order), so beams also fire from copies' spots.
8. **Remembered-spot beams shaped like Marisa's, smaller.** They now start narrow and widen the way Marisa's boss beams do (same start width, growth power 3 and peak factor, all scaled), at a quarter of Marisa's size throughout (`BYAKUREN_SPOT_BEAM_SIZE` 0.5 → 0.25; before, they were a constant half of her widest). The big star on each spot is scaled by the same factor.
9. **No shake; attack beams are headless Sekibanki trails.** None of her beams shake the screen any more (`LaserEmitter.NoShake`). Her attack beams are no longer rotating lasers: when an attack begins, five invisible heads start from the pentagon round her, away from the player, and home like Sekibanki's heads (bat turn rate) for 16 beats (`BYAKUREN_TRAIL_LIFE_BEATS`), leaving a beam of normal slider width; every slice is there for 4 beats (`BYAKUREN_TRAIL_STAY_BEATS`) and then disappears instead of flying away. It hurts like a slider body (`HeadAct.Beams`, the head act now takes a style). Her spinner keeps its spinning laser beams (no shake).
10. **No beam sound.** Her lasers (spinner beams and remembered-spot beams) make no beam sound (`LaserEmitter.Silent`); her trails never had one.
11. **Homing beams live like Koakuma's bats.** Her headless homing beams no longer stop after 16 beats (`BYAKUREN_TRAIL_LIFE_BEATS` unused): each goes on until it has been within 2 osu! circle radii of the player for 8 beats in all (`KOAKUMA_BAT_FADE_RADIUS_CIRCLES`, `KOAKUMA_BAT_FADE_BEATS`), or has left the playfield (60 s at most, the bats' limit). Slices still vanish 4 beats after they're laid.
12. **Remembered spots throw homing beams.** The small Master Sparks from the remembered spots are gone: 32 beats after every fifth circle or slider (at least 2 beats after the spot before) five homing beams like hers start from a pentagon round that spot, away from the player (`BossPeriod.SpotThrows`, `HeadAct` style `FromSpots`). The big star on the spot went with the Master Sparks.
13. **The big star is back, briefly, and hurts.** When a remembered spot throws its beams, the big star in her colours flashes up there: it fades in over 250 ms, stays for 1 beat (`BYAKUREN_SPOT_STAR_BEATS`), fades out over 250 ms, spinning. While it is mostly there it hurts like a slider body (its body, at the bullet hitbox share). It covers every Byakuren section now (it used to be registered for her first one only).

## Hong Meiling (`Qi`)

### 2026-10-18

1. **Every second pulled bullet flies on.** Of the bullets she draws in (counted in the order she takes them), every second one is still fed into the ball (it counts for its size, speed and colour) but does not disappear: when it reaches her it flies on through her the same way at its own speed (not the pull speed), harmless while it is drawn in and hurting again from there (`StageBullet.GhostUntil`, fully visible).
2. **Every second bullet is left alone instead.** Replaces 1. Of the bullets that come within her reach (counted in the order they do), every second one is not drawn in at all: it is not fed into the ball and flies on as it was. The others are drawn in and fed into the ball as before.

## Urumi Ushizaki (new mechanic `Momentum`, no longer a placeholder)

### 2026-10-19

1. **Pushes.** No pauses (`pause` 0 like Zanmu). Every 8 beats (`URUMI_CYCLE_BEATS`) from the start of her section a push is announced (`MomentumAct`, `BossOps.PushMarks`). For 4 beats (`URUMI_PREVIEW_BEATS`) every bullet on the screen, and every bullet shot meanwhile, becomes a bullet of the act: light grey (`URUMI_GREY` 0.75), glowing (`URUMI_BLOOM` 0.8), swelling to 2x (`URUMI_SIZE`, hitbox too) over 300 ms, shaking 1-2 px. A faint grey copy (30 % visible, `URUMI_GHOST_VISIBLE`) shows where each will be 4 beats after the push. Then every one of them gets an extra straight velocity of 0.5x the section's bullet speed (`URUMI_PUSH_SPEED`, adjustable in the boss menu as "Push speed") in the direction boss -> player as it was when the announcement began. Pushes add up, also for bullets caught earlier (`PushPlan`: prefix sums, the offset is a closed form of time; `StageBullet.Pushes/PushFrom`). Beams are not touched.
2. **Own bullets.** Ellipses (they fall towards the player like Toyohime's) in rings of 20 (`URUMI_RING_BULLETS`), one ring every 2 beats (`URUMI_RING_EVERY_BEATS`). The spinner's stars are replaced by the same rings of 20 ellipses. The spinner's beams are gone (they are for every boss but Marisa).
3. Benchmark (Chirality [Asymmetry] 3:00): Urumi alone 4.3 ms per frame, with Lily White 4.8, with Sakuya 6.3, with Zanmu 8.4. No crashes.

## Lily White (`Spring`, no longer a placeholder)

### 2026-10-19

1. **Branches and flowers.** Eight branches grow from the four corners and the middles of the four sides, inwards, each as big as a sub-branch of Yuyuko's tree (`LILY_BRANCH_LENGTH` 0.24 and `LILY_BRANCH_WIDTH` 0.028 of the tree's size, with the sub-branches that come out of it: 15 per anchor), drawn as wood and hurting like a slider body (`LilyAct`). In her attacks every bullet that leaves the screen at any side grows a flower on the branches, evenly spread (the farthest from the flowers there of 8 tried places, `LILY_FLOWER_CANDIDATES`; was: on the branch nearest to where it left) as often (`YUYUKO_SOW_SHARE`) and as fast (`YUYUKO_PETAL_GROW_BEATS`) as Yuyuko's. Each flower bursts into its 5 petals (pink petal bullets) 8 beats after it began to grow (`LILY_FLOWER_BEATS`), not all at the end of the attack. In pauses no flower grows; the others keep growing and burst on time. When she goes the branches grow backwards; a flower still there bursts then.
2. **Own bullets.** Rings of 12 flowers (`LILY_RING_BULLETS`), 2x the size of the flowers that fall from Yuyuko's top (`LILY_FLOWER_SIZE`, new `BulletEmitter.FlowerScale`), every 2 beats, every second ring turned half a step. They flash (the glow pulses 16 times, brighter and brighter, `LILY_FLASH_BLOOM`; `BulletEmitter.Flashes`) and after 8 beats burst into 5 petals (`BulletEmitter.RemoveAfter`, plus 5 `DeriveKind.Explode` rings made out of the flower ring, `makeLilyFlower`). The spinner's stars are replaced by the same rings; its beams are gone.
3. Benchmark: Lily White alone 1.65 ms per frame; with Cirno 32 ms (Cirno's 29k bullets; Sakuya + Cirno is about the same). No crashes.

## Shinmyoumaru Sukuna (new mechanic `Inchling`, no longer a placeholder)

### 2026-10-19

1. **Sizes by distance.** In her attacks every bullet and every beam (slider and spinner beams of the map; hitboxes too) is `SUKUNA_MIN_SIZE` 0.5x at her centre, rising linearly to `SUKUNA_MAX_SIZE` 4x at a distance equal to the playfield's width and beyond. The glow (bloom) goes up the same way, 0 at her to `SUKUNA_BLOOM` 0.9 at the full distance. `InchlingAct` only sets `BulletField.sukunaLevel` / `sukunaCentre` / `sukunaShaking`; the draw (ring bullets, stage bullets, beam widths and per-beam bloom) and the hit checks (rings, stage bullets, beams) read `sukunaGrow(p)`.
2. **Warning.** The level goes from 0 to 1 over the warning before each attack (as long as the warning before her first attack), stays 1 during the attack, and goes back to 0 over as long after it ends. Everything (ring bullets and stage bullets) shakes 1-2 px during the warning. Beams do not shake.
3. **Sound.** Kyouko's wall sound (`kyouko_walls`) at the start of every attack.
4. **Sizes changed** (later 2026-10-19): `SUKUNA_MIN_SIZE` 0.5 → 0.25, `SUKUNA_MAX_SIZE` 4 → 3.
5. Her own bullets stay the default stars (nothing was asked); no beams in her spinner (it is a rule for every boss). Benchmark: 1.1 ms per frame alone, 3.0 with Sakuya, 8.6 with Zanmu, 16 with Cirno. No crashes.

## Ichirin Kumoi and Unzan (new mechanic `Cloud`, no longer a placeholder)

### 2026-10-19

1. **Pictures.** The user's Unzan picture: white background removed (flood fill from the border, soft edge) and cut into `Resources/Textures/unzan_head.png`, `unzan_hand_left.png`, `unzan_hand_right.png` (the head and the right hand touch in the picture: cut by eroding the mask and growing the three pieces back; a small flame curl of the right hand's top left is part of the hand). New `StageTexture.UnzanHead` (layer under the bullets) and `UnzanHandLeft/Right` (layer above them).
2. **Head** behind her (at her place, 4.6 circle radii wide, nudged and tilted up to 25 degrees towards the player).
3. **Throws.** In her attacks (`IchirinAct`) every 8 beats (`ICHIRIN_THROW_EVERY_BEATS`) one hand, left and right in turn, is thrown at the player: a quadratic arc that bulges outwards (`ICHIRIN_ARC` 0.35 of the way's length), speeding up (position goes with the time to the power 1.6, `ICHIRIN_ACCEL_POWER`), 2 beats, to the wall behind the player (the line from her through the player, as it was at the throw; Mokou's wall margin). It stays 8 beats, returns in a straight line in 2 beats. It hurts (1.3 circle radii) while it is out. The throw is made once when it starts and thrown away on a rewind (like Mokou's legs).
4. **Impact.** Mokou's landing rings (`MOKOU_RINGS` 20 x `MOKOU_RING_BULLETS` 8, her spiral and flash, no big circle) at the hit point, the Moon's hit sound of Hecatia (`hecatia_moon_hit`), the field shakes 1-2 px for 4 beats (`ICHIRIN_SHAKE`, `ICHIRIN_SHAKE_BEATS`).
5. **Clap.** In a pause, once both hands are home (at the earliest 1.5 beats into the pause), they come together 3 circle radii in front of her (towards the player) in 1 beat and clap: 4 rings (0.5 beat apart) of 21 ellipses whose directions and speeds make the tip of an arrowhead (`RingShape.Triangle`, aimed at the player), the same shake and the same sound; they go home in 1 beat. If it does not fit into the pause there is no clap.
6. **Chains.** A hand that is home is the free end of a chain of 8 nodes from her (the verlet chain of Hecatia's worlds: same damping and lag), 35 % longer than needed, shaken with a little noise every step and pulled home weakly, so it is unsteady; the hand's picture turns with the last link. While a hand is out the chain's end is held on it (the chain follows as an arm of faint pink puffs). The chains are simulated from the section's start in 4 ms steps and thrown away on a rewind.
7. She shoots nothing else: no own rings, the spinner's stars are gone. Benchmark: 0.9 ms per frame alone, 5.5 with Sakuya, 4.9 with Zanmu, 2.2 with Hecatia.

### 2026-10-19 (later)

**Clap rings are an arrowhead's outline.** Instead of the triangle layout: 28 ellipses (was 21) whose speed follows `ICHIRIN_ARROW_BACK` 0.35 + 0.65 x (1 - angle from the aim / 180 degrees) ^ `ICHIRIN_ARROW_SHARPNESS` 2.4, so the ring is a sharp tip towards the player with round arc sides and a round back, and keeps that shape as it grows.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 2)

1. Impact: half the rings (`ICHIRIN_IMPACT_RINGS` 10, was Mokou's 20 of 8).
2. Twice the size, hands included: `ICHIRIN_HEAD_WIDTH` 4.6 → 9.2, `ICHIRIN_HAND_HEIGHT` 4.2 → 8.4, `ICHIRIN_HAND_HIT` 1.3 → 2.6 (the hit too), home place 2.1/1.5 → 4.2/3, clap distance 3 → 6, arm puffs 2x.
3. Half the speed: throw `ICHIRIN_THROW_BEATS` 2 → 4, return `ICHIRIN_RETURN_BEATS` 2 → 4.
4. Heavier: chain noise `ICHIRIN_CHAIN_NOISE` 0.25 → 0.05, pull home 0.012 → 0.02.
5. Warning: before a throw the hand is drawn slowly back (smooth step, `ICHIRIN_WINDUP_BEATS` 1.5, `ICHIRIN_PULLBACK` 4 circle radii) along the line it will fly on (the direction is chosen when the windup starts) and does not hurt meanwhile. To keep a hand's round at 16 beats (windup 1.5 + throw 4 + stay + return 4) the stay at the wall is `ICHIRIN_HOLD_BEATS` 8 → 6.5.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 3)

1. **No chain physics.** The chains (and the arm puffs along them) are gone (`ICHIRIN_CHAIN_*` removed). A hand that is home swings a little, slowly, as a closed form of time: `ICHIRIN_SWING` 0.3 circle radii once per `ICHIRIN_SWING_BEATS` 3 beats, turning `ICHIRIN_SWING_DEGREES` 5.
2. **Warning.** The act starts with the warning (`period.Start`), not the first attack. The head grows from nothing to its size and the hands fade in over as long as the warning (at least 300 ms); when her phase ends it goes the other way over as long. The windup of a throw now ends at the attack time (`Ready` = attack time - `ICHIRIN_WINDUP_BEATS` 1.5; the throw starts at the attack time), so the first hand is already drawn back in the warning; a hand's round is still 16 beats.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 4)

1. **Hands no longer come back just as they are thrown again.** A hand's round was exactly 16 beats, so the next windup began the very beat it got home (and with a tempo change it could begin before). Now a throw waits until its hand has been home for `ICHIRIN_REST_BEATS` 1 (the windup and throw come later if need be), and the rounds are shorter: windup 1.5 + throw + stay + return + rest ≤ 16.
2. **Half the throw speed:** `ICHIRIN_THROW_BEATS` 4 → 8. To keep the round (1.5 + 8 + 2 + 3 + rest): the stay at the wall `ICHIRIN_HOLD_BEATS` 6.5 → 2, the return `ICHIRIN_RETURN_BEATS` 4 → 3.
3. **Head.** Always behind her as seen from the player (its middle `ICHIRIN_HEAD_BEHIND` 0.3 of its width away from the player) and turned all the way to face the player (before: in front, tilted at most 25 degrees).
4. **Less swing:** `ICHIRIN_SWING` 0.3 → 0.1 circle radii, `ICHIRIN_SWING_DEGREES` 5 → 2.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 5)

1. **Hands swapped.** In the clap the left hand went to the right side (the line across the aim pointed the wrong way for a downward aim): now the left hand always meets the right on its own side.
2. **Flicker.** The turn of a hand jumped at every change (swing 2 degrees to the throw's angle, the throw's tangent, the way home - opposite to the way it flew - back to upright), and the swing started and stopped at once. Now every pose is continuous: the turn goes by the short way from one pose to the next (`lerpAngle`), and the swing fades in and out with how much the hand is at rest (`calm`).
3. **Hands near the head.** Before the first attack the hands stay close to the head (`ICHIRIN_NEAR_X` 2.2, `ICHIRIN_NEAR_Y` 1 circle radii) and move out to their places (4.2, 3) over the warning; when her phase is over they go back to the head as the cloud goes (`spreadAt`).
4. **Sharper pictures.** The source is scaled 2x (Lanczos) and sharpened, and only the darkest ink is pushed towards black (below 95 of 255 brightness, power 2.2; a stronger setting made the shading muddy): the textures are 548x513 (head) and 336x631 / 367x686 (hands), were 276x260 and 172x317 / 186x345. The drawn size is the same.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 6)

**A throw cannot be interrupted.** Before, a throw whose landing was after the end of her phase was cancelled (no impact) and everything faded at the end. Now a throw always runs through windup, flight, stay and return, with its impact rings and sound; the cloud stays until the last hand is home (`finish`: the end of her phase or the last `ReturnEnd`, whichever is later), and the fade-out, the hands' return to the head and the hurting last until then. (The clap still only happens inside her phase.)

### 2026-10-19 (later, 7)

1. **Inner arc.** The arc's side was chosen by the sign of the line's x direction, which is only right for a mostly vertical throw. Now the arc bulges to the side of the line that is farther from her (her place vs the arc's middle), whichever way the hand is thrown.
2. **Walls behind her.** The arc's control point and the place the hand is drawn back to are clamped into the playfield (the same margin as the landing), so a hand no longer goes out through, or is drawn back through, the walls behind her.
3. **Mono fill.** The pink areas of the pictures are two tones now (the average light and the average shadow tone, blended by brightness, 85 %); the ink lines are untouched.
4. **Head swing.** The head sways like the hands, even more slowly (1.5x the period) and as little (`ICHIRIN_SWING`, `ICHIRIN_SWING_DEGREES`).

### 2026-10-19 (later, 8)

**Hands never land on the walls behind her.** The landing was the first wall the line from her through the player reached, so when the player was on her side of the screen the hand hit the wall right behind her as soon as it was thrown. Now the two walls nearest to her (the nearest side and the nearest top or bottom) are ignored: the hand lands on one of the two far walls (`landingOf`). If the line to the player does not reach a far wall inside the playfield, the parts of the direction that point at the walls behind her are turned round (the hand is thrown towards the far side of the screen instead), and the windup goes along that line. The earlier clamp of the arc into the playfield stays.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 9)

1. **Hands at rest face the player** (like the head), not always down: windup, return and clap now turn from / to the way to the player instead of upright.
2. **Bigger outer arc:** `ICHIRIN_ARC` 0.35 → 0.7 of the way's length (still inside the playfield).

### 2026-10-19 (later, 10)

**The whole cloud turns towards the player.** The hands' resting places (and the places they stay near the head in the warning) were fixed left and right of her on the screen while the head and the hands' pictures turned to the player, so with the player at the side both hands stood on the same side of the head. Now all places are in the cloud's own frame: `facing` = the way from her to the player; the head is behind her on that line, the hands are to the left and the right of it (and a little in front), and the clap's two sides are the same. While the player is within 2 circle radii of her the last facing is kept, so the cloud does not spin round when he is on top of her.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 11)

1. **Slow clap, on the player's spot.** The hands come together over `ICHIRIN_CLAP_BEATS` 4 beats (was 1) and go home in as many. They set off when both are home (half a beat after, at the earliest half a beat into the pause) for the place where the player is at that moment (kept inside the playfield), and it does not change after; the rings go out of that place, aimed away from her. A clap needs 8 beats and a half in the pause or there is none. The hands hurt while they are about to meet, as they meet and a little after (from 30 % of the approach before to 20 % of the return after).
2. **Wider arc:** `ICHIRIN_ARC` 0.7 → 1.2.
3. **Rounder back of the arrowhead ring:** `ICHIRIN_ARROW_BACK` 0.35 → 0.55, `ICHIRIN_ARROW_SHARPNESS` 2.4 → 2 (the back was close to the middle where the bullets lie close together and look pointed).
4. **Flat cartoon pictures.** The fill is now only the light tone or the shadow tone with a narrow step between them, decided on a blurred copy (no paper speckle or halftone dots); the edge of the ink keeps its own darkness so the outline has no halo.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 12)

**White specks and fringe gone from the Unzan pictures.** (1) The flat fill skipped everything brighter than 226, so white specks inside the shapes (and next to the ink) stayed: now it is applied to every kept pixel, after the background is known, with the blurred brightness capped at 215 so the white of the background does not pull the edge pixels to the light tone. (2) The light fringe outside the outlines (what the JPEG left between the ink and the white): kept pixels brighter than 195 that touch the background become background, five rounds.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 13)

**Unzan pictures redone as a three-colour cartoon.** The picture is no longer touched up pixel by pixel (that left shadow speckles, uneven outlines and thin white lines at the edge). Now every pixel is one of three classes - ink (almost black), shadow, light (the average tones of the picture) - decided on a blurred copy (the background counts as ink there), then a majority filter (radius 3 then 2, ink counted 1.8x so thin ink lines stay) swallows every speck and halftone dot and evens the outlines. The pixels within 3 px of the background are always ink, the background is transparent black, and a slight blur (0.8 px) anti-aliases the edge, so there is no white line at the edge. Sizes 549x515 (head), 338x626 / 367x686 (hands).

### 2026-10-19 (later, 14)

1. **The clap is held.** After the hands have met they stay together `ICHIRIN_CLAP_HOLD_BEATS` 2 beats (`Clap.Release`) before they go home (4 beats). They hurt until the release. A clap now takes 4 + 2 + 4 = 10 beats and must fit into the pause: the hands set off a quarter beat after both are home (was half), so with a 16 beat pause and a hand thrown at the middle of the attack it ends at about beat 31 of 32. A longer hold would not fit and there would be no clap.
2. **Snap at the impact.** A hand's turn jumped when it hit the wall (the flight's last tangent -> the straight line of the throw, and again when it set off home). Now the turn the hand has as it hits (`Toss.HoldTurn`, the last tangent of the arc) is kept while it stays and is where the return turns from.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 15)

**The clap's arc.** The hands now go to the clap along an outer arc (a quadratic curve whose middle is pushed outwards, away from the other hand, by `ICHIRIN_CLAP_ARC` 1.0 of the way's length) instead of a straight line, and speed up much more at the end (position goes with the time to the power `ICHIRIN_CLAP_ACCEL_POWER` 3, the throw has 1.6): slow at first, fast as they meet. They go home along the same arc.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 16)

1. **Clap acceleration halved:** `ICHIRIN_CLAP_ACCEL_POWER` 3 → 1.5, so the speed at the meeting is about half of what it was (the clap's length cannot be doubled: it would not fit into the pause).
2. **Unzan aims at the real cursor.** While the cursor is frozen (the character stays where it froze) the cloud used the character's place: the head and hands facing, the way and the landing of a throw, and the place of the clap. Now they use the real cursor (new `BulletField.cursorPosition` / `aimPosition`, set every frame before anything is simulated). The hands still hurt by the character's own place (the hitbox does not move). Other bosses still aim at the character; only Ichirin was changed.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 17)

**Layers of Unzan.** From the bottom: her magic circle, the hands, the head, then the bullets, then her. Before, the circle (part of her drawable, above the bullets layer) was above the head, and the hands were in the front layer above everything (hit objects and her too). Now the head and the hands are both in the layer under the bullets (`stageBack`, the hands drawn first), and on maps with Ichirin her magic circle and its glow are taken out of her drawable (`BossCharacter.CreateCircleProxy`, drawn in `circleSlot` below that layer). Other bosses are not changed. The hands are now under the bullets and the hit objects too.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 18)

**Layers of Unzan, corrected (replaces 17).** The cloud is only to be directly below her, nothing else. Now the order from the bottom is: the bullets and everything else as before, then her magic circle (drawn from a proxy in `circleSlot`, on maps with Ichirin), the hands, the head (a new layer `stageCloud`, hands drawn first), then her. The head and the hands are no longer in the layer under the bullets (`stageBack`) nor the hands in the front layer: they are above the bullets and below the hit objects (like her).

### 2026-10-19 (later, 19)

1. **Ichirin: one warning.** Like Mokou, the warning before her section (the power-up sound and the stars that fly in to her) is the only one; the warnings before her later attacks are gone (`BulletField` `once`, `BossCharacter` `windows`).
2. **Luna Child: the bullets' own edges are smooth too.** Besides the two soft copies, a third (1.15x, 55 %) and the bullet's own body is see-through (`LUNA_BODY_SEETHROUGH` 0.4 x blur), so its sharp edge is not what the player sees; all of it scales with the distance as before. The cap of bullets that get copies is 4000 (was 6000: four instances per bullet now; above it only the glow).
3. **Luna Child: arcs halved.** `LUNA_ARC_SHARE` 0.6 → 0.3 (an arc is 30 % of its share of the circle: the gaps are twice as wide as the arcs now).

### 2026-10-19 (later, 20)

**Luna Child: only the edge smoothing, no cap.** The big soft halos (1.4x and 2.2x copies) and the cap of 4000 bullets are gone (`LUNA_SOFT_SIZE_1/2`, `LUNA_SOFT_ALPHA_1/2`, `LUNA_SOFT_CAP` removed). What is left, for every bullet whatever their number: the see-through body (`LUNA_BODY_SEETHROUGH`), one soft copy under it (1.15x, 55 %) and the glow, all by the distance from her. Two instances per blurred bullet instead of four.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 21)

**Luna Child: the edges are gone.** At full blur the bullet's own body is 85 % see-through (`LUNA_BODY_SEETHROUGH` 0.4 → 0.85) so its outline cannot be seen, and there are two soft copies under it again (`LUNA_SOFT_SIZE_0` 1.2 at 45 %, `LUNA_SOFT_SIZE_1` 1.55 at 22 %), so what is seen is a soft glow that fades out. Still no cap; three instances per blurred bullet.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 22)

**A third hand.** Every section of Ichirin has its own cloud (`IchirinAct`), and a cloud stays until its last hand is home and then goes away over the length of the warning, so where one section follows another (break, kiai, spinner) the old cloud was still there while the next one's warning had brought a new one in: two heads and up to four hands at once. Now a cloud gives way to the next one of the same boss: from the moment the next one starts it fades out over half a warning and is gone (it neither draws nor hurts), so there is never more than one cloud (`NextStart`, set in `registerStage`). A throw that was still going when the next section starts is cut then (the hand fades with the cloud); its rings that are already out stay.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 23)

**Luna Child: half see-through, still smooth.** The body of a blurred bullet is only half see-through at full blur (`LUNA_BODY_SEETHROUGH` 0.85 → 0.5), and the smoothness is kept by three soft copies under it instead of two (`LUNA_SOFT_SIZES` 1.1 / 1.28 / 1.55 x the bullet, `LUNA_SOFT_ALPHAS` 60 / 40 / 20 %; were 1.2 and 1.55 at 45 and 22 %), so the edge fades in small steps. Four instances per blurred bullet.

### 2026-10-19 (later, 24)

**Luna Child: really soft bullets, no layers, not see-through.** The copies under the bullets and the see-through body are gone (`LUNA_SOFT_SIZES`, `LUNA_SOFT_ALPHAS`, `LUNA_BODY_SEETHROUGH` removed): the bullets are as opaque as everyone's again. Instead the renderer has a second, soft sheet (`BulletRenderer.makeSoft`): every cell's picture shrunk to half in the middle of its cell and blurred (6 % of the cell), so a bullet drawn from it twice as big has its own size and an edge that fades into nothing. `BulletInstance.Soft` (0..1) fades the sharp picture into the soft one; Luna sets it by the distance from her (full from half the way out, `LUNA_SOFT_RAMP` 2), the glow stays. One quad per bullet (two while it is between sharp and soft). Knives, talismans, flowers and trains have no soft picture and stay sharp.

## Sukuna, sizes again

### 2026-10-19 (later)

`SUKUNA_MIN_SIZE` 0.25 → 0: the size multiplier is 0 at the edge of her magic circle (and inside it: nothing is drawn or hit there) and rises linearly to 3x at a playfield's width from that edge (was: from her centre). ## Rule for every boss that stops being a placeholder

### 2026-10-19

When a placeholder gets its own mechanic: the spinner's beams are removed (the planner already does that for every boss but Marisa) and the spinner's stars are replaced by the boss's own bullets (`planSection`, the spinner replacement). Applied to Urumi and Lily White.

## Koakuma (`Bats`)

### 2026-10-18

1. **Wider fade-out zone.** A bat fades once it has been within 2 osu! circle radii of the player for 8 beats in all (was 1 radius; `KOAKUMA_BAT_FADE_RADIUS_CIRCLES` 1 → 2). Byakuren's homing beams use the same rule.

## Doremy (new mechanic `Dream`, no longer a placeholder)

### 2026-10-18

1. **Mirrored dark blue copies.** Every circle and slider that starts in one of her attacks is shown again, point-mirrored through the playfield centre (x and y), with combo colour dark blue (`DOREMY_COPY_COLOUR`), no sound. They're real osu! circles/sliders (`DoremyMirrorLayer`), hit automatically like the originals, but not counted in score, combo or health. They're drawn above the bullets and under the real objects. Slider copies don't hurt on touch.
2. **Copies shoot.** Every ring of the map's circles, sliders and spinner fired in her attacks gets a copy fired from the mirrored spot, every bullet the opposite way (rings that aim at the player aim at the player's mirror image), half the size, twice the speed, darker (`DOREMY_COPY_SIZE/SPEED/SHADE`). Slider beams are not copied.
3. **Spinner.** Her spinner has no beams (already the case); the spinner's stars are plain bullets and are copied the same way for the whole spinner.
4. **Her own shots.** Plain bullets: two continuous chains of 3-bullet rings, turning opposite ways (`DOREMY_CHAIN_BULLETS`), at the spinner spiral's rate (half in kiai).
5. **Copies hurt like the originals.** A copied circle hurts the player standing in it when it's hit, and a copied slider body hurts while it's active, exactly like the originals: mirrored circle and slider zones are added for the objects that start in her attacks (`PlanCircleZones` / `PlanSliderZones` with `DoremyMirrorLayer.Windows`). This replaces "slider copies don't hurt" in 1.
6. **Copy bullets retuned.** Back to the original's brightness (`DOREMY_COPY_SHADE` 0.4 → 0: a copy keeps whatever shade its original had) and 75% of the size instead of half (`DOREMY_COPY_SIZE` 0.5 → 0.75). Still twice as fast.
7. **No pauses.** Her 16-beat attacks follow each other without pauses, so her copies run for the whole section.
8. **Half as many copied bullets.** Every copied ring keeps every other bullet (`BulletEmitter.Halved`; a ring of one stays one). In a two-boss section the usual halving applies on top.
9. **No combo numbers.** Her copied circles and slider heads hide their combo numbers; a decorative white bullet (white body, light grey rim) of the bullet radius for the map's circle size sits in their place, fading with the circle (`CloneLayer` marks). Sagume's copies keep their numbers.

## Sagume Kishin (new mechanic `Rewind`, no longer a placeholder)

### 2026-10-18

1. **Attacks replayed backwards.** As soon as each attack phase ends, its circles and sliders are played again in reverse order, at the same positions (no mirroring): an object that started t ms before the attack's end starts again t ms after it, so the replay fills the pause after the attack. Approach circles play normally. A slider that ended at the far end of its path starts there and runs back along it; one with an odd number of repeats ended at its start, so it runs the same way. Copies are white (`SAGUME_COPY_COLOUR`), silent, hit automatically, not counted in score, and hurt like the originals (circles and slider bodies).
2. **Copies shoot.** Every ring of the map's circles, sliders and spinner fired in her attacks is fired again at the mirrored time (2 × attack end − t), from the same place, the same way (rings that aim at the player still aim at the player), white (new `TINT_WHITE`), 2× the size, 4× slower (`SAGUME_COPY_SIZE`, `SAGUME_COPY_SPEED`).
3. **Shared with Doremy.** Doremy's copies and Sagume's are now made by one `CloneLayer` (replaces `DoremyMirrorLayer`); the damage zones are built from the copy objects themselves (`PlanCircleZones` / `PlanSliderZones` take the copies).

4. **Copy bullets retuned.** 1.5× the size instead of 2× (`SAGUME_COPY_SIZE` 2 → 1.5), 2× slower instead of 4× (`SAGUME_COPY_SPEED` 0.25 → 0.5).
5. **No pauses.** Her 16-beat attacks follow each other without pauses; each attack is still replayed backwards right after it ends, so the replay now plays over the next attack.
6. **Half as many copied bullets.** Every replayed ring keeps every other bullet (`BulletEmitter.Halved`; a ring of one stays one). In a two-boss section the usual halving applies on top.

## Sekibanki (new mechanic `Heads`, no longer a placeholder)

### 2026-10-18

1. **Homing heads.** Every 8 beats of each attack (from its start) she throws a head from where she is, flying straight away from the player at her plain bullets' speed. It homes like Koakuma's bats (same turn rate: arrow gravity × `KOAKUMA_BAT_TURN_FACTOR`, constant speed), is a bullet 2× her plain bullet's size, lives 64 beats, then stops and fades out over 500 ms (`HeadAct`, `SEKIBANKI_*`).
2. **Picture.** Drawn as her animated head from the supplied GIF: 12 frames (400/50 ms, as in the GIF), each centred on its own bounds so the GIF's shake is gone, cut to a square and scaled to 128 px (`Resources/Textures/sekibanki_head_0..11.png`). Drawn 1.3× the hitbox wide so the face fills the hitbox. The source's faint PICMIX watermark is still in the frames.
3. **Beam trail.** Behind each head is a beam 2× a slider beam's width, in her colour, glowing. Every slice stays where the head was for 32 beats, then flies on the way the head went there at slider-beam speed and is gone once off the playfield. It hurts like a slider body.
4. **Sound.** Yuyuko's petal sound (`yuyuko_petals`, the sound her tree throws its flowers with) plays every time a head is thrown.
5. **Every beam keeps its head.** A head used to be used up (and vanish) when it hit the player, while its simulation went on laying beam without it. Heads are now never used up: they hurt like a slider body (while touching, every so often), like their beams, so a beam always has its head.
6. **Trail stays half as long.** Slices stay where the head left them for 16 beats instead of 32 (`SEKIBANKI_TRAIL_STAY_BEATS` 32 → 16), then fly off.

## Reisen (`Lunatic`) and Sakuya (`TimeStop`)

### 2026-10-18

1. **No old-position previews.** Reisen's frozen bullets no longer leave a purple copy where they were frozen; only the red copy where they will slide to remains. Sakuya's bullets that her mirroring will move are no longer tinted grey where they are; only the green copy where they will be put remains.
2. **Slides are not cut short.** Their attack windows are no longer clipped at the end of the section (like Nue's), so a freeze/slide or a time stop always runs its full length even when the section ends first. Reisen's short beams still stop at the section end.
3. **Slides are not cut short by other bosses either.** A slide used to end early when another boss's op began during it (that op took the bullet from there); now it always runs to its own end (`Trajectory.Trace`).
4. **Reisen: every second bullet is left alone.** Her freeze-and-slide skips every odd bullet of a ring (`TrajectoryOp.EveryOther`; `Trace` now gets the bullet's index); those fly on as if nothing happened. Applies to ring bullets that fly straight; her beams' slices and pulled/ricocheting bullets (which freeze by their own simulation) still all slide/freeze.
5. **Dim and warnings only with slides.** The slide happens only in every second attack, but the purple background dim, the frozen-bullet fade, the sideways blur, the power-up warning sound and the warning stars came with every attack. They now all come only with the slide attacks (`BossOps.ReisenSlides`, shared by the ops, the field's windows and the warning stars); the generic per-attack warning skips her. The freeze and release sounds (Yuyuko's petals at the start and end) too: they played at every attack and now only at the slide attacks.

## Performance

### 2026-10-18

1. **Rings that were only frozen are dropped once they are off the screen.** Reported: Reisen + Doremy freezes the game on 7★+ maps in intense parts. Cause: a freeze op (Reisen's slide, Cirno's freeze) is put on nearly every ring alive at its start (7944 of 8405 rings on Chirality with both), its lifetime is stretched (×3 + freeze time + 2 s), and a ring with ops was never dropped early by the "past every corner of the screen" check, so thousands of rings stayed alive and every bullet was traced through its ops every frame. Now a ring whose ops are only freezes (and slides) counts as radial again once the last one is over: its slowest bullet's radius minus the frozen time and the slide distance is checked against the screen (`BulletEmitter.GoneAfterFreezes`). Measured with Tools/SectionCheck `bench` (bullet positions only, Chirality Asymmetry, 3:00–3:30, both bosses in every section): average 3.4 → 1.4 ms per frame, worst 8.7 → 6.1 ms, rings alive at the worst moment 2300 → 1498. Reisen alone: 3.4 → 1.5 ms average (her worst moment, during a slide, stays ~12 ms: everything frozen is really on the screen).
2. **Rings that fell as petals are dropped too.** Reported: Doremy + Yuyuko and Doremy + Sunny Milk freeze the game in intense parts. Yuyuko's Fall op (what leaves the top falls as petals) kept every ring alive for its stretched lifetime; it now counts like a freeze for the off-screen check (its fall delay is taken off the radius). Bench (Chirality, both bosses): 2.54 → 1.06 ms average per frame.
3. **Stage bullets skip what is gone.** Stage acts (Sunny Milk's prism splits pile up thousands of them) were walked from their first bullet every frame, for drawing and for hits. Each act now keeps a cursor past the bullets that are gone for good (`StageAct.SkipGone`, `DrawFrom` / `HitFrom`), reset when time goes back.
4. **Headless field benchmark.** New `field` mode in Tools/SectionCheck: plans a map like the playfield (two bosses in every section), loads a real `BulletField` without audio or skin and runs its `Update` at 60 fps over a stretch of the song with a 16:9 screen (singles, every pair, or given combinations). It prints ms per frame (average, 99th percentile, worst), allocations, and the most expensive steps of `Update` (`BulletField.ProfileTicks` / `PROFILE_STEPS`: a null check when unused), plus live rings, bullets, beams and beam points. All numbers below: Chirality [Asymmetry] 3:00, before → after, average (worst) ms per frame.
5. **Okina: beams through the portals.** Every slice of every beam was followed in 24 ms steps through the whole time a portal was open, every frame (and again 24 times per corner search). A slice that nothing else steers flies straight, so it can only enter a portal when it reaches the wall: that moment is worked out directly and the portals are asked once (`carryStraight`). Okina 105 (662) → 2.9 (5) ms. This was a real freeze.
6. **Clownpiece: beams into the moon.** Every beam point walked its whole flight back (up to 200 steps) every frame to see whether it had touched the moon. Now each slice remembers how far back it has been checked and whether it is gone (`moonSlices`, cleared on seek), so only the newest part of its flight is walked. 13.6 (27) → 3.1 (5) ms.
7. **Rings with ops are traced once per frame.** Rings that are not checked as a whole (ops, bursts, bounces) were traced three times per bullet per frame (collision start and end, drawing). Now a per-ring snapshot (`RingSnapshot`, pooled) keeps where each bullet was: the collision check starts from last frame's end, the renderer and the stage scan reuse what the collision check worked out, and a frozen bullet (a freeze that doesn't slide or blend its look) is not traced again until its freeze ends (`TrajectoryState.StillUntil`). Sakuya 13.0 → 3.5, Seija 10.0 → 6.0, Yukari 10.1 → 6.9, Reisen 7.6 → 5.4.
8. **Rings with ops are dropped once their ops are over.** They used to live their whole stretched lifetime (×3 + freeze time + 2 s). Once the last op of a ring is over its bullets fly straight, so the moment the last one leaves the screen is worked out once and the ring is dropped then (`GoneAfterOps`; not for bouncing, falling, pulled, stage-turned or petal rings). Burst rings that fly straight from their own origins (Cirno's) are dropped like plain rings once past every screen corner (`IsStraightFromOrigins`). Cirno 12.4 → 6.6; Sakuya + Cirno 79 (138) → 29 (70): this pair still keeps ~47 000 bullets alive, which is what the two mechanics make.
9. **Zanmu's beams.** Between two stopped slices (both grey steps) no corner is searched for any more (it was searched between nearly every pair of points), the corner search takes 16 halvings instead of 24, a beam is sampled slice by slice only once one of her ripples can have stopped part of it (`IBeamFreezer.CanHaveStopped`), and a beam that has left the screen whole with no stopped slice is finished like any other. 7.6 → 6.9 ms (her stopped slices on the screen are real work).
10. **Smaller things.** `RemovedAt` and the ops' end are worked out once with the ops instead of every call; the stage scan (UFOs, black hole, planets...) carries each bullet's place from one step to the next instead of working it out twice. Utsuho 12.7 → 7.7, Hecatia 5.2 → 3.8.
## Boss sections

### 2026-10-18

1. **Sections for maps that have too few.** If a map's breaks, kiai sections and spinners give the bosses no sections, or less than 25% of the song (first object to end of last object), the song is cut into 16-beat stretches (beat length from the timing where each starts) and the most intense ones become kiai-type boss sections until 25% is covered. Intensity = average of three measures, each as a share of the map's highest: average loudest hitsound volume of the objects, song loudness (from the track's waveform), fastest slider velocity. Stretches that already hold a section, or have no objects, are skipped; neighbouring chosen stretches merge into one section. The song is only decoded when filling is needed; if it can't be read, the other two measures decide (`BOSS_MIN_SONG_SHARE`, `BOSS_FILL_BEATS`, `SongLoudness`, `BulletPlanner.Sections`). The boss picker's HP bar preview uses the same sections.
2. **Fill to half.** Still triggered below 25%, but the chosen stretches are now added until the bosses cover half the song (`BOSS_FILL_SONG_SHARE` 0.5).
3. **Trigger at half too.** Any map whose own sections cover less than half the song is now filled up to half (`BOSS_MIN_SONG_SHARE` 0.25 → 0.5).
4. **Object density.** A fourth intensity measure: objects per ms in the stretch (as a share of the densest stretch). The score is now the average of four measures (three when the song can't be read).
5. **Priority.** The measures are weighted: hitsound loudness 0.4, song loudness 0.3, object density 0.15, slider speed 0.15 (`BOSS_FILL_*_WEIGHT`). Without the song, the other three keep their ratio.
6. **Average frequency.** A fifth measure from the song: the average frequency of each stretch, from the waveform's bass/mid/treble band intensities (band centres 60 / 700 / 6000 Hz), intensity-weighted on a log scale; higher frequency scores higher, scaled from the song's lowest stretch to its highest. Weighted the same as hitsound loudness (`BOSS_FILL_FREQUENCY_WEIGHT` = 0.4). Weights now: hitsounds 0.4, frequency 0.4, song loudness 0.3, density 0.15, sliders 0.15 (divided by their sum). Without the song, frequency and loudness both drop out.
7. **Bands instead of loudness and average frequency.** Song loudness and the single average frequency are removed. The song now counts as three measures, the average strength of each waveform band in the stretch (each as a share of the strongest stretch): treble 0.4 (same as hitsound loudness), bass 0.3, mid 0.2. Weights now: hitsounds 0.4, treble 0.4, bass 0.3, mid 0.2, density 0.15, sliders 0.15 (divided by their sum); without the song the bands drop out. `SongLoudness` is renamed `SongBands`.
8. **Drums.** Kicks (bass band) and snares (mid band) are detected as onsets in 10 ms frames: a frame where the band jumps up more than 2× the average jump within ±0.5 s, and is the local peak, at least 50 ms after the previous hit of that band. Each hit's strength is its jump relative to the band's average level. A stretch's drum measure is half hit rate (hits per ms) and half average hit strength, each as a share of the map's highest. Weighted 0.35, between treble and bass (`BOSS_FILL_DRUM_WEIGHT`). Weights now: hitsounds 0.4, treble 0.4, drums 0.35, bass 0.3, mid 0.2, density 0.15, sliders 0.15.
9. **Drums are short bursts.** A hit must also fall back by at least half its jump within 150 ms of its peak, so only short strong bass bursts (and snare bursts) count, not the start of a long bass note. The waveform's bass band is 20–100 Hz (osu! framework constants: low 20–100, mid 100–2000, high 2000–12000 Hz); 100–200 Hz sits inside the mid band and cannot be separated without decoding the audio ourselves.
10. **Half-time drums.** The strongest drum hits (top 25% by strength) are analysed for their usual spacing: the most common gap between consecutive strong hits, in 10 ms steps between 150 and 2000 ms. Strong hits whose gap to the strong hit before or after them is twice that (±12%) are half-time hits: same strength, half the frequency (a dubstep drop). A stretch's half-time measure is half their rate and half their average strength, each as a share of the map's highest, weighted the same as the drums (`BOSS_FILL_HALF_TIME_WEIGHT` = 0.35). Needs at least 8 hits and 4 strong ones, otherwise it counts nothing.
11. **Distance between objects, and new priorities.** New measure: the average distance from where each object ends to where the next one starts (spinners skipped), as a share of the map's highest. Weights now: drums 0.5; hitsounds 0.4, treble 0.4, half-time drums 0.4; bass 0.3; distance 0.25, slider speed 0.25; mid 0.2; density 0.15 (divided by their sum).
12. **Drums and half-time from the most common hit frequency.** The usual spacing is now the most common gap between consecutive drum hits of all strengths (not only the strongest 25%). The drums measure counts only hits at that usual spacing (gap to the hit before or after ±12%); the half-time measure counts hits at twice it. Both still score half rate, half average strength.
13. **32-beat minimum, quiet parts.** Stretches are now 4 beats (`BOSS_FILL_STEP_BEATS`, was 16). A quiet part is a run of stretches where bass + mid + treble together are below 35% of the song's median stretch level (without the song: stretches with no objects) lasting longer than 8 beats. Sections grow from the most intense free stretch towards whichever neighbour scores higher (joining an already chosen section next to it) until they are 32 beats long (`BOSS_FILL_MIN_BEATS`). A section never enters a quiet part; it may stay shorter than 32 beats only when a quiet part stops it, otherwise (blocked by the map's own sections or the song's ends) it is dropped. Filling still stops once half the song is covered, but a section that has started always reaches its minimum. Applies to the chosen sections only; the map's own breaks, kiai sections and spinners keep their old minimum.
14. **Stretches on the beat grid; analysis hook.** Stretches now start on the timing point's 4-beat grid (a new timing point starts its own grid) instead of at the first object, so section edges can land on the music's phrases. The measuring was moved into a public `BulletPlanner.AnalyseStretches` (no change in behaviour) so maps can be analysed offline.
    - Analysis of Camellia - Chirality (Dissymmetric Long ver.) [Asymmetry] and [Expert] against the wanted sections 0:48–1:07, 1:36–2:34, 2:57–4:05 (scratchpad tool `sectioncheck`): the current algorithm picks only 0:51–1:01 because the map's own sections already cover 47% and filling stops at 50%; the wanted sections are ~70% of the song. Per-stretch separation (AUC): song level 0.956, hitsound 0.947, treble 0.948, mid 0.923, bass 0.846, slider 0.798, density 0.785, distance 0.58, half-time 0.45, drums 0.41 (the onset detector fails on this loud compressed mix). The wanted edges are all on 16-beat phrase starts. A phrase-based rework (16-beat phrases on the grid, three-level Otsu threshold keeping the top level, holes of ≤ 2 phrases filled, runs < 2 phrases dropped, no coverage target) with weights hitsound 0.4, song level 0.4, treble 0.35, mid 0.3, bass 0.2, slider 0.15, density 0.15 reproduces the wanted sections exactly on both difficulties. Not applied yet: proposed to the user.
15. **Phrase-based rework (applied).** The fill-to-a-share greedy picker, the 50% fill target, the quiet-part rule and the 32-beat grow are gone. Now (`BulletPlanner.IntenseParts`): stretches of 4 beats on the beat grid are scored with hitsound 0.4, song level (bass + mid + treble) 0.4, treble 0.35, mid 0.3, bass 0.2, slider speed 0.15, density 0.15 (drums, half-time drums and distance are still measured but not scored); stretches are grouped into 16-beat phrases of the grid and averaged; three-level Otsu splits the phrases into calm / build-up / intense and only intense is kept; gaps of ≤ 2 phrases between intense parts are filled; parts shorter than 2 phrases (32 beats) are dropped. Still triggered only when the map's own sections cover < 50% of the song. A map kiai section that an intense part overlaps or touches (within a beat) is merged into one section with it; breaks and spinners keep their own bosses and intense parts are cut round them (pieces shorter than the break minimum are dropped). Result on Chirality: 0:48–1:07, 1:36–2:33, 2:57–4:05 on Asymmetry, Expert, Insane and Hard. The analysis tool is kept at `Downloads\Danmakusu\Tools\SectionCheck` (`dotnet run -- <map.osu> <audio> <out.csv> "m:ss-m:ss,..."`).
16. **Weights tuned on two songs.** USAO - Cyaegha (wanted 0:22–1:00, 1:38–2:17) was overshot with the Chirality weights (0:12–1:00, 1:14–2:16): the song is loud throughout, so song level / treble / mid barely separate there (AUC 0.56 / 0.67 / 0.33), while hitsound (0.96), distance (0.84) and slider speed (0.83) do. A grid search over all weight combinations (Tools/SectionCheck `search` mode, steps 0–3, 262144 sets) on Chirality Asymmetry/Expert/Insane + Cyaegha Drop's Extra/0922_'s Expert/Avarice gave: hitsound 0.3, song level 0.2, bass 0.2, drums 0.1, everything else 0. Result on all 12 difficulties of both songs: Chirality 0:48–1:07, 1:36–2:33, 2:57–4:05 (Easy: last part from 2:52); Cyaegha 0:21–0:55, 1:33–2:16 (one phrase short at 0:55–1:00, which holds a spinner, and one phrase early at 1:33).
17. **Third song, new measures, hill climbing.** USAO - Blows Up Everything (wanted 0:19–0:55, 1:22–1:40, 1:45–2:12; no kiai, 215 BPM) failed with the previous weights (0:01–0:54, 1:17–2:10 on most difficulties): its intro has full-volume hitsounds and the loudest drums, and its 1:40–1:45 pause is only softer hitsounds (≈86%). New measures (analysis): additions per object, finishes per ms, stream share, slider share, map kiai, bass share of the level, and rise (level against the 16 beats before; build-ups get louder as they go). New hole rule: a gap is only filled when every phrase in it scores ≥ `BOSS_FILL_HOLE_SHARE` (0.9) of the threshold. SectionCheck got `auc`, `eval` and `climb` (hill climbing from 400 random starts, negative weights allowed, cost = average error + worst/4) modes and `maps.ps1` listing the examples. Chosen (not the lowest average, but the one that gives nearly the same sections on every difficulty of a song): hitsound 3, bass 1, bass share 1, drums −1, rise −2, hole share 0.9; average error 6.1%, worst 9.8% over all 20 difficulties. Results: Chirality 0:38–1:07 (2 phrases early), 1:36–2:33, 2:57–4:05 (Easy: 1:36–2:14); Cyaegha 0:12–1:00 (2 phrases early), 1:38–2:16 (now exact); Blows Up Everything 0:19–0:54, 1:21–2:11 (the 1:40–1:45 pause is not found; OTOSAKA's ReMASTER+ gets a gap at 1:30–1:35).
18. **Fourth song, phrases weighted by duration, new weights.** Camellia - epimerization (789927 [catalyst], wanted 0:49–1:58, 2:34–3:21, 3:49–4:05, 4:08–4:51; 0:30–0:40 optional, counted as neither right nor wrong: SectionCheck targets take `~m:ss-m:ss` for optional ranges). Phrases are still 16 beats on the grid, but a scrap left by a tempo change is merged into its neighbour and a phrase's score is its stretches' average weighted by their duration (`BulletPlanner.Phrases`). The picker is now `BulletPlanner.PickIntense` (three-level `Otsu`, holes, minimum length) so the tool runs exactly the game's code. Climbed again over all 21 difficulties of the four songs. Weights (`BOSS_FILL_*`) old → new: hitsound 3 → 4, song level 0 → 1, treble 0 → 1, mid 0 → −2, bass 1 → 3, slider speed 0 → 1, density 0 → 2, distance 0 → 2, drums −1 → −1, additions 0 → 2, finishes 0 → −2, streams 0 → −1, slider share 0 → 1, rise −2 → −2, bass share 1 → 0, kiai 0. Average error 6.1% → ≈5.3%.
19. **Build-ups become spinners.** The phrases of the middle Otsu level (build-up) right before an intense part, at least 16 beats long (`BOSS_FILL_BUILD_UP_MIN_BEATS`), become spinner-type sections, but only where the map has no objects (cut round every object and every other section). A build-up has the same boss as the drop that follows it (it takes that section's slot) and doesn't count as a section of its own for the boss order; the kiai counter isn't advanced by it.
20. **Breaks are cut round objects.** A break section (map break or generated) is cut where hit objects (other than spinners) fall in it, so no break ever covers circles or sliders; pieces shorter than the break minimum are dropped.
21. **Second epimerization map (830986 [J1 x Frontier's Equatorial]): why it was a mess.** The song analysis gives nearly the same intense parts as on the first map. The difference was the map: it puts short spinners inside the drops, and spinners kept their own bosses, so every drop was cut into many short pieces (some dropped as too short) with spinner sections between them. Now a map spinner that an intense part overlaps is absorbed into the part (like a touching kiai section). Result: 0:28–1:57 kiai, 1:57–2:03 spinner, 2:04–2:10 break, 2:28–3:21 kiai, 3:30–4:50 kiai, plus a few spinners in the gaps. Remaining error: the first and third parts start with the build-up (0:28 instead of 0:49, 3:30 instead of 3:49), which this map makes as dense as the drop.
22. **(2026-10-30) Two more songs: nothing changed, and why.** Added to `maps.ps1`: USAO - Dynamite (Camellia's MACHO TNT REMIX) [DARKNESS] and Camellia - Operation Zenithfall [Final Mission] (the user's "4:44 - 5:02 - 5:50" read as one part 4:44-5:50). Dynamite was already right with the weights of 18 (5.6 % of its stretches wrong; the game picks 0:18-0:36, 0:53-1:28, 1:36-1:53, 2:11-2:28, 2:50-3:24, 3:29-4:10). Zenithfall is 33.9 % wrong: 0:28-0:44 and 3:10-3:40 are picked and not wanted, 2:51-3:10 and 5:16-5:50 are wanted and not picked. Tried, all rejected: (a) `climb` on the 12 listed difficulties: best set 6.1 % average, Zenithfall 15 %, but `eval` on all 23 difficulties gives 12.8 % against the current 6.5 % (Blows Up Everything [BASIC] 55 % wrong): badly overfitted; (b) `climb` on all 23 from the current weights (`CLIMB_SEED`, new): the best by the cost has Zenithfall 13.5 % and worst 21 %, but the average goes up to 8.1 % and epimerization loses 0:49-1:36; (c) new rhythm measures (how much the kick beats on the beat, the snare on every second, the hi-hats off the beat, flux; Fourier of the band at that period): AUC on Chirality 0.34 / 0.20 / 0.35 / 0.38, Cyaegha 0.76 / 0.62 / 0.79 / 0.77, Blows Up Everything 0.41 / 0.53 / 0.64 / 0.53, Zenithfall 0.49 / 0.46 / 0.41 / 0.48: no signal that holds across songs, taken out again. The reason (Zenithfall's csv): the unwanted 3:12-3:40 scores like the wanted parts or above them on every measure (hitsound 0.96, treble 0.62, level 0.73 against 0.85, 0.63, 0.75 for the wanted 2:51-3:12) and the wanted 5:16-5:50 like the unwanted ones (slider 0.28, density 0.31): the three bands of the waveform and the map's objects do not tell them apart. It would take something that hears the music itself. The weights stay.

## Boss picker

### 2026-10-18

1. **HP bar preview.** Under the Search box of the boss picker (mod settings and the boss menu before a map), the selected map's boss HP bar is shown full, coloured by the bosses that would be played (checked ones; the combo colours fill the rest), without names or heads. Built from `BulletPlanner.Sections` / `BarPeriods`, so no bullets are planned.
2. **Names in two rows.** The name next to each head is split into two rows at the space that makes the rows most even (a one-word name stays one row), so long names are no longer cut off.

## Field

### 2026-10-18

1. **No hit-cause text.** The small debug text under the miss marker where the player was hit (what hit them: ring, bullet number, stage act...) is gone; only the skin's miss judgement shows.

## Sunny Milk (prism)

### 2026-10-18

1. **Smaller diffracted bullets.** The split bullets are 3× smaller than the bullet that hit the prism instead of 2× (`SUNNY_SPLIT_SIZE_DIVISOR` 2 → 3), hitbox included.

## Patchouli (`SevenElements`)

### 2026-10-18

1. **Water slows longer.** Bullets the water ripples touch are 8× as slow instead of 4× (`PATCHOULI_WATER_SLOWDOWN` 4 → 8) and take 16 beats to get their speed back instead of 8 (`PATCHOULI_WATER_RECOVER_BEATS` 8 → 16).
2. **Metal adds knives.** The metal ripple no longer turns bullets into knives: the bullet flies on and a knife is put on top of it (once per bullet), frozen for 8 beats pointing at the player, then flying that way at the bullet's speed.
3. **More, slower leaves.** Each tree tip carries two leaves instead of one, side by side and turned slightly apart, so 10 leaves per tree instead of 5 (`PATCHOULI_LEAVES_PER_TIP` = 2), thrown at half the speed of the bullet that grew the tree (`PATCHOULI_LEAF_SPEED` = 0.5).
4. **Water: every second caught bullet rides the ripple.** Of the bullets a water ripple catches (counted per ripple), every second one is put on the middle of the ripple's band and flies outwards with it at the ripple's speed, blue, so the riders move together as a ring. Their places spread evenly over the part of the ripple that is on the screen at that moment (golden-ratio sequence over the visible arc, so the spread stays even however many it catches). The others are slowed down as before. Riders are ordinary stage bullets: a hit uses them up.
5. **Trees glow and bloom.** The wood of her trees has a warm light core and a bloom halo under it (`PATCHOULI_TREE_BLOOM` 0.9, new: wood beams can bloom now; Yuyuko's tree and Remilia's attack leave wood as it was), and the leaves bloom as much.
6. **Riders appear as ghosts.** A bullet put on a water ripple is see-through (35% visible, `PATCHOULI_RIDE_GHOST_VISIBLE`) and harmless for its first 4 beats (`PATCHOULI_RIDE_GHOST_BEATS`), then turns solid over 200 ms (new `StageBullet.GhostUntil`).
7. **Earth: circle-sized stones, full-size splinters.** A stone is as big as an osu! circle (`PATCHOULI_EARTH_SIZE` 2 bullet radii → 1 circle radius; its hitbox keeps the bullet's share), and the five bullets it bursts into are as big as the bullet the stone was (were half the stone's size).
8. **Riders: a ghost first, then on the ring.** Replaces 6. A caught bullet that will ride the ripple first goes on as it was (same place, same flight) for 4 beats, harmless and 35% visible, fading out over the last 60% of that time. Then it fades in (200 ms) on the middle of the band where the ripple has got to by then, at its place of the even spread (worked out over the part of the ripple on the screen at that moment), and rides it outwards.
9. **Riders: the bullet stays, its clone rides.** Replaces 8. The caught bullet behaves normally (same flight, harmful) for 4 beats, while a clone of it is already on the ring at its place of the even spread, blue, 35% visible and harmless, riding outwards. After the 4 beats the bullet is gone and the clone turns solid (over 200 ms) and hurts.
